# Receptor integration: adversarial verification record

# Round 1

Nine skeptic / critic agents re-checked the round-1 deliverables (`docs/audits/receptor_sources_*.md`, `receptor_rules.md`,
`receptor_nt_disagreements.md`, `receptor_integration.md`, the tables in `flyverse/data/`, `connectome.receptor_signs`,
`LIFParams.receptor_model`). Their verdicts are recorded here verbatim; **the corrections below have NOT yet been applied to
the shipped tables** -- round 2 (`docs/NT_INTEGRATION.md`, section 7) applies them and re-scores. Read the round-1 numbers
in `receptor_integration.md` with these caveats.

## verify:tables:ozel2021  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* Pm1 at tier 'exact' (cluster 163 -> MaleCNS Pm1 only): Nern 2025 Sup_Table_7 (data/external/nern2025/nature_esm/MOESM4_unzipped/Sup_Table_7_MatchingCellTypes_final.xlsx) matches FlyWire/Schlegel 'Pm1' (245/231 cells) to THREE MaleCNS types, Pm1 (121), Pm5 (94, Matsliah Pm02) and Pm6 (24, Matsliah Pm06); the 2020-era Pm1 (Erclik 2017 marker definition used by Özel) is therefore a MaleCNS class exactly like Pm2 -> Pm2a/Pm2b, which the agent did tier as 'class'. By its own rule cluster 163 should be class over {Pm1, Pm5, Pm6} (MaleCNS: Pm1 246 cells / 169,388 out-syn; Pm5 182 / 147,555; Pm6 50 / 77,713 -- Pm5 even appears in the report's own 'largest unmatched types' list). Exact-tier count 46 and the exact/class coverage split are inflated by this row.
* Hemibrain-name bridge claim 'Pm1-4 keep hemibrain names (hemibrainType column)': the MaleCNS hemibrainType column is EMPTY for Pm1, Pm2a, Pm2b, Pm3, Pm4 (and for every mapped ol_intrinsic type); the Nern 2025 Sup_Table_7 hemibrain_type column is also None for these rows. The Pm bridge rests only on Nern 2025 reusing the names; Pm3 in particular is FlyWire CB3856 / Matsliah Pm09 (no FlyWire 'Pm3' at all).
* 'mAChR-C (CG7918) is not in the BDGP6.88 gene set the authors used': CG7918 is absent from the 12,028-gene Adult sheet, which is the authors' expressed-gene set after filtering, not the BDGP6.88 annotation (which contains ~17k genes incl. CG7918). The correct statement is 'not detected / filtered out of the per-cluster tables'.
* 'expression_ozel2021_mm.csv: same shape, 199 clusters x 54 genes': the Adult_MM_final sheet has only 198 cluster columns -- cluster 192 (LQ) is absent -- so row 192 of the mm CSV is all-NaN; the table has 198 clusters with data, not 199.

### Corrections to apply
* Re-tier cluster 163 Pm1 as 'class' over Pm1, Pm5, Pm6 (FlyWire 'Pm1' is a 3-way split in MaleCNS per Nern 2025 Sup_Table_7), or at minimum add Pm5/Pm6 with a flag; this moves 232 cells / 225,268 output synapses from unmatched to class and 246 cells / 169,388 syn from exact to class; update the tier counts (exact 45 types) and the coverage tables accordingly.
* Tm29 'exact' rests on the name only: FlyWire calls the same MaleCNS cells Tm5d (Matsliah) / CB3851 (Schlegel, which also lumps Tm39 and Tm40, 373 cells), and no hemibrain name exists; Özel's cluster 55 matched a 2018 driver-line called 'Tm29'. Whether that driver labels Nern-2025 Tm29 is not evidenced in the sources used (the paper text has no 'Tm29' sentence; only ED Fig. 3). Keep the row but demote to 'fuzzy' or add an explicit name-collision flag (transmitter Glu agrees on both sides, which is weak support).
* Dm11 'exact': Schlegel Dm11 = MaleCNS Dm11 (158 cells) + Dm-DRA2 (33 cells, 7,110 out-syn) per Sup_Table_7; note it as a minor split (does not change fractions materially).
* Rewrite the mAChR-C note: 'CG7918 is not among the 12,028 genes retained in the authors' per-cluster tables' rather than 'not in the BDGP6.88 gene set'.
* State that the mixture-model table has data for 198 clusters (cluster 192 LQ has no column in Adult_MM_final and is NaN in expression_ozel2021_mm.csv), and that the per-cluster mm value is the model's P(ON) for the cluster, distinct from the sheet's freq_cell_ON (fraction of cells with >= 1 UMI); the CSV header's '~fraction of cells ON' gloss should be dropped.
* The nomenclature paragraph should not cite the hemibrainType column for Pm1-4 (it is empty for them); cite Nern 2025 Sup_Table_7 (Pm1 = Schlegel Pm1 / Matsliah Pm01, Pm2a = Pm2 / Pm03, Pm2b = Pm2 / Pm08, Pm3 = CB3856 / Pm09, Pm4 = Pm4 / Pm05).
* Not applicable to this task and not verified here: receptor_model=None byte-identity, slow-term sign convention, tests and probe reruns -- this report contains no model code or scores (the modified brain.py/optic.py/probe scripts in the working tree belong to other tasks). No GPU or cluster work was needed or run.

### Confirmed
* All SHA-256 and byte sizes recomputed locally and match (Log_normalized 145,820,714 / 44999534...; Mixture_modeling 48,796,225 / 76deacac...; Cluster_markers 35,990,473 / 6415901f...; MOESM4 35,904 / 17e7b13f...; soft.gz 4,501 / 27124d72...). GEO FTP listing (fetched 2026-09-11) confirms Adult.rds.gz 3.5G, Adult_male.rds.gz 58M, pupal 601M-1.1G, dates 2019-12-18..31.
* Adult sheet: 12,028 genes x 199 cluster columns (ids 1..235; absent ids 2,25,35,88,89,97,121,122,127,130-135,141,143,169,193-195,209-220; 12 backprojection columns 221,222,225-232,234,235) -> 193 final clusters is consistent (199 - 12 + 6 replaced originals). Gene list identical between the two GEO sheets; no duplicate symbols.
* Gene symbols: 50 of 54 plan names present as-is (all ten nAChR subunits already carry nAChRalpha1-7/beta1-3 symbols, no nAcRalpha-96Aa forms; ort, HisCl1, Oamb, mGluR, GABA-B-R1-3, 5-HT1A/1B/2A/2B/7, Dop1R1/1R2/2R/EcR, Octbeta1-3R present); ChAT = Cha, KaiR1D = CG3822, Octalpha2R = CG18208 present under the old symbols; CG7918 / mAChR-C absent. So 53/54 is correct.
* CSV values re-read from the raw sheets for 12 genes (Cha, CG3822, CG18208, VGlut, ort, Oamb, nAChRalpha1, 5-HT7, Hdc, GluClalpha, Rdl, mGluR) across all clusters: max |csv - raw| <= 1e-16 for both the log-normalised and the mixture-model tables; mm columns correctly re-indexed despite the sheet's shuffled column order and 11 trailing model columns.
* Paper (PMC7790857) confirms verbatim: 109,743 adult cells after QC; female Canton-S dissected within 3 days of eclosion; male barcodes removed by MSL-gene UMIs; 'LC10b transcriptome correlated best with cluster 79, but also ... 93, 78 (LC10d) and 77 (LC10a) ... cluster 93 likely being LC10c'; 'LC16 matched best with cluster 100, but with a correlation gap of only 0.03'; 'Pm3 transcriptome correlated best, but weakly, with cluster 151'; '193 final clusters'; '172 clusters as optic lobe neurons'; '61 of our 172 neuronal clusters'; BDGP6.88 / CellRanger 2.1.0; gap >= 0.05 criterion. GEO SOFT: 38 samples, 15 adult libraries, Canton S, 1-3 days, 13 female + 2 'pooled male and female'.
* ST1 (MOESM4): 436 rows incl. header, 261 cluster rows; every one of the 199 adult clusters has a row; 87 annotated adult clusters = 62 mapped-with-annotation + 25 named-unmatched (TmY8*, 20 G*/LQ, 88a/b, 169a/b); the ST1 sentences quoted in the evidence column for clusters 1, 3, 19, 27, 55, 60, 64, 70, 75, 100, 104, 111, 137, 163, 175, 222, 225-228, 234, 235, 93, 98, 102, 112, 191, 192, 221 match the sheet; no annotated adult cluster with a MaleCNS type name was left unmapped; TmY8 absent from MaleCNS type, flywireType and hemibrainType columns.
* Type map arithmetic: 224 rows, 199 clusters, 88 pairs over 84 types; exact 46/46, alias 0, fuzzy 6 clusters/7 types, class 11/31; 136 unmatched. All MaleCNS type names exist in cache/neurons.parquet; flywireType bridges confirmed (Tm29 -> Tm5d 544/544, Dm3a/b/c -> Dm3p/q/v, Dm8a/b -> yDm8/pDm8, LC10c-1/-2 -> LC10c, LC14a-1/-2 -> LC14a1/a2).
* Coverage recomputed independently from the CSV + cache: optic lobe 104,689 cells, 628 types, |W| out 51,923,264 (float32 rounding vs reported ...269/268), in 49,414,460; exact 44,658 cells / 22,276,748 out / 15,664,813 in (42.7 / 42.9 / 31.7 %); fuzzy 2,214 / 1,697,167; class 27,251 / 6,882,604; unmatched 544 types, 30,566 cells / 21,066,750 (40.6 %). Per-superclass tables (ol_intrinsic 47.9/47.4 %, visual_projection 20.0/19.7 % exact, 17.2/16.6 % fuzzy, 331 unmatched types, ol_sensory class 99.9/99.4 %, visual_centrifugal 108 types 0 %) all reproduce; mapped types occur only in ol_intrinsic / ol_sensory / visual_projection.
* Both-ends matrix reproduced entry-for-entry (exact-exact 8,524,355; unmatched-unmatched 13,618,561; etc.): both matched 15,732,833 (30.3 %), exact/fuzzy both ends 9,731,080 (18.7 %), pre matched 30,856,518 (59.4 %).
* Top unmatched types reproduced (Y3 627 / 560,136; Tm5Y 898 / 438,245; Tm6 367,913; TmY17 316,097; TmY15 310,732; TmY10 295,718; Tm37 292,254; TmY18 287,435; Tm12 282,916; TmY13 268,481).
* Transmitter cross-check reproduced: 74 of 88 agree; the 14 disagreements are T1, the 10 photoreceptor rows (no synthesis gene ON; PR Hdc log-mean 0.50, mm 0.00; T1 Hdc 0.001) and LC14a-1/a-2/b (cluster 27 VGlut P(on) 1.00, ChAT/VAChT 0.00 vs MaleCNS acetylcholine 100 %); TmY14 cluster 137 VGlut 1.00 vs MaleCNS glutamate 44 % / ACh 37 % / unknown 19 %. Nern 2025 Sup_Table_1 also predicts ACh for LC14a-1/LC14b and Glu for TmY14, so the LC14 discrepancy is atlas vs both EM predictions.
* Cluster_markers_Adult sheet 51,393 rows; ST2 1,408 rows; MOESM7 zip contains the Keras appendix (model*.h5, Means*.npy, Markers*.rds); sizes of MOESM1-7 match the report.

## verify:tables:davis2020  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* CLAIM 'All 57 panel genes present under FlyBase 2017_04 symbols; KaiR1D = CG8916': REFUTED on both counts. (a) The panel in flyverse/data/expression_davis2020.csv has 54 genes (12 synthesis/transport + 10 nAChR + 3 GABA-A + GluClalpha + 5 iGluR + HisCl1/ort + 3 mAChR + 3 GABA-B + mGluR + 4 Dop + 5 Oct + 5 5-HT = 54; 54 *_tpm and 54 *_p_on columns, 115 columns total, not '57 genes'/'114 columns'). (b) KaiR1D is NOT CG8916. NCBI Gene 42473 KaiR1D ('Kainate-type ionotropic glutamate receptor subunit 1D') aliases = Dmel_CG3822, and Davis table 3 lists CG3822 = FBgn0038837 (KaiR1D's FBgn) while CG8916 = FBgn0030707, which NCBI Gene 32553 describes as 'uncharacterized protein', alias Lcch-14A (a Cys-loop ligand-gated channel homolog, not an iGluR). The parallel Özel task (scripts/build_ozel2021_tables.py SOURCE_SYMBOL) correctly used KaiR1D = CG3822, so the two sources disagree on the same column. Consequence: the KaiR1D_tpm / KaiR1D_p_on columns in expression_davis2020.csv carry CG8916 expression (Dm10 190.5, Mi15 155.8, Tm4 145.9, L1 121.0, Mi4 115.0, T4 114.3, Mi1 1.6, LC4 1.1 TPM), whereas true KaiR1D (CG3822) in table 4 is broad and flat: Dm10 75.3, Mi15 70.7, Tm4 89.9, L1 45.6, Mi4 76.2, T4 78.6, T5 84.5, Mi1 45.9, LC4 66.1, Tm9 107.3, Tm2 115.1, Lai 2507.4 (range 0.8-2507). Downstream: flyverse/data/receptors_by_type.csv (davis2020 is SOURCE_PRIORITY 0) has 102 davis2020 glutamate rows; the mislabeled gene is fast_pos_lead in 6 and fast_pos_lead_nonmda in 15 (Dm10, Dm4, L1, L2, Mi15, Mi9, T4a-d, Tm2, Tm20, Tm3, Tm4, Tm9), so the iGluR gain classes / nonmda net signs for those types are computed from the wrong gene.
* CLAIM 'Receptor panel: KaiR1D 0.6-191 (highest Dm10 191, Mi15 156, Tm4 146, L1 121, Mi4 115, T4 114; Mi1 1.6, LC4 1.1) ... iGluR share highest in Dm10 / Tm4 / L1 / T4 / Mi4 / Mi15': REFUTED as a statement about KaiR1D -- those numbers are CG8916 (Lcch-14A), see above. With the real KaiR1D (CG3822) the ranking is Lai 2507, Tm2 115, LPLC2 112, LLPC1 108, Tm9 107, Lawf2 99, T1 94, TmY5a 90, Tm4 90, Dm11 88, and the 'GluCl-dominated' conclusion for T4/T5/LC4 still holds but the 'iGluR share highest in Dm10/Tm4/L1/T4/Mi4/Mi15' list does not.
* CLAIM 'T1 (MaleCNS histamine, Davis Hdc 0.6 TPM, nothing > 5 TPM)': partly REFUTED. Table 4 T1 column: Hdc 0.61, ChAT 3.04, VAChT 3.63, Gad1 1.59, VGlut 2.48, ple 1.34, DAT 2.39, Trh 4.51, SerT 1.17 -- but VGAT 172.6, Tbh 5.75, Tdc2 5.49 TPM. 'Nothing > 5 TPM' is false (VGAT 173, Tbh 5.8, Tdc2 5.5); the softer statement 'no synthesis enzyme > 5 TPM' is what the data support (VGAT is 200-300 TPM in L1/L2 too, so it is not diagnostic).
* CLAIM '12 unmatched (... neuropeptide / enhancer-trap drivers: Kdm2, NPF, Crz, CCAP, Dsk, Ilp2 -- no MaleCNS type label for a neuropeptide population)': REFUTED for four of the six. cache/neurons.parquet has types IPC (insulin-producing cells = the Ilp2-GAL4 population), CRZ01 / CRZ02 (Crz), DSKMP3 (Dsk), NPFL1-I (NPF). Ilp2 -> IPC in particular is a clean class/alias-level match; the tables leave them unmatched. Only CCAP and Kdm2 (enhancer trap) have no MaleCNS counterpart among type names.

### Corrections to apply
* Rename/relabel the iGluR column: expression_davis2020.csv 'KaiR1D_tpm' / 'KaiR1D_p_on' must be rebuilt from CG3822 (FBgn0038837 = KaiR1D), not CG8916 (FBgn0030707, 'uncharacterized protein' / Lcch-14A); fix the gene-symbol note in docs/audits/receptor_sources_davis2020.md section 2 and the KEY CLAIM; then rebuild flyverse/data/receptors_by_type.csv (scripts/build_receptor_table.py; 15 davis2020 glutamate rows have the wrong gene as nonmda lead: Dm10, Dm4, L1, L2, Mi15, Mi9, T4a-d, Tm2, Tm20, Tm3, Tm4, Tm9) and re-derive any receptor_rules.md / receptor_integration.md statements about the iGluR share ranking (true KaiR1D is flat 30-115 TPM across optic types, 2,507 in Lai).
* Panel size is 54 genes, not 57 (expression CSV is 100 x 115: 4 meta + 54 tpm + 54 p_on + 3 call columns), and the doc's '100 rows x 114 columns' is off by one.
* QC-pass-only coverage has a small bug: types reached by both a pass and a suboptimal-only source (R7p via R7 vs R7_Rh3; PAM08 via PAM_4 vs PAM_2/PAM_6) were dropped. Correct values: optic QC-pass 61 types, 61,409 cells (58.7 %), 48.0 % out-syn, 35.6 % in-syn (report: 60 / 61,077 / 58.3 % / 47.9 % / 35.5 %); both-ends optic 22.6 % (report 22.5 %); cb_intrinsic QC-pass 20 types, 2,096 cells (6.5 %), 2.9 % out (report 19 / 2,046 / 6.4 %); whole CNS QC-pass 38.0 % cells / 21.4 % out (report 37.8 % / 21.4 %). Type counts of 629 optic / 6,606 cb / 557 unmatched include an untyped-NaN pseudo-type; actual named types are 628 / 6,605 / 556.
* T1 disagreement wording: replace 'nothing > 5 TPM' with 'no synthesis enzyme > 5 TPM' -- VGAT is 172.6 TPM (as in L1 304.6 / L2 216.7, so non-diagnostic), Tbh 5.75, Tdc2 5.49.
* Unmatched list: MaleCNS has IPC (Ilp2), CRZ01/CRZ02 (Crz), DSKMP3 (Dsk), NPFL1-I (NPF); at least Ilp2 -> IPC should be a class-tier row, and the doc sentence 'no MaleCNS type label for a neuropeptide population' should be limited to CCAP and Kdm2.
* Whole-CNS matched cells are 38.8 % (64,893 / 167,106), not 38.9 %.
* Minor evidence-string inaccuracies in type_map_davis2020.csv: Lat row cites 'Tuthill 2013 lamina tangential' but supp. 1A gives 'Dionne et al in preparation' for SS00657; lLNv row cites 'Helfrich-Forster 2007' but supp. 1A says 'a gift from Heather Dionne, Rubin lab'. T4 / T5 evidence correctly records the driver bias ('strongest in T4b,T4c'; T5_d1 'strongest in T5c,T5d', T5_d2 'mainly T5a,T5b'), so the T4/T5 pooled profiles should be flagged as subtype-biased when used per subtype.

### Confirmed
* Downloads: all 12 files present in data/external/davis2020/, 39,371,853 bytes total; every SHA-256 in docs/audits/receptor_sources_davis2020.md matches sha256sum (README fbd0e7cb..., table1 b96dd43d..., table3 5c035c18..., table4 90f3ae41..., table7b 8f827bdd..., supp1 8f789047...). GEO listing https://ftp.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/series/GSE116nnn/GSE116969/suppl/ shows the same 11 files (2018-07-11); tables 4 and 7b re-downloaded now hash identically (90f3ae41..., 8f827bdd...).
* Table shapes: table 4 = 13,931 genes x 79 cell columns; 7a/7b = 12,377 genes x 77 cells (7b + 9 model columns = 87 cols); table 3 = 13,931 x 266 samples with gene_id/gene_name. The 79 names listed in section 3 of the doc match the table-4 header exactly; 7b lacks 'lamina' and 'opticlobe' as claimed. Note table 4's gene index contains the gene 'nan' (nanchung), which pandas reads as NaN -- harmless for the panel.
* QC: supp. 1B has 266 samples, 203 pass / 63 suboptimal; exactly 21 non-control cell types have only suboptimal samples and the list matches the report verbatim (CCAP, Dsk, Ilp2, KC_apbp_ap, KC_apbp_m, Lat, LC10bc, LC10d, lLNv, LPTC_HS.VS, MBON_bp1, MBON_g1pedc, PAM_2/5/6/7/8/9, PB_6, Pdf, R7_Rh3). Two drivers (Dm12_d1, NPF_d1) mix pass and suboptimal samples.
* PB_3 contamination note is verbatim in supp. 1A ('many OL cells including Mi1; also GF cells; OL cells outnumber cells in PB'); PB_1 = 'PB18.s_Gx_7Gy.b__PB18.s-9i1i8c.b' (SS00116), PB_2 = 'PBG1-8.b-EBw.s-D_Vgall.b' (SS00090, ref. Wolff and Rubin 2018), PB_3 = 'PBG2-9.s-EBt.b-NO1.b' (SS02268), PB_4 = 'PBG2-9.s-FBl1.b-NO3P.b' (SS02204/SS02302), PB_5 = 'PBG2-9.s-FBl2.b-NO3A.b' (SS02255), PB_6 = 'PBG2-9.s-FBl3.b-NO2D.b' (SS00078). PB_1 VGlut 4,113.3 TPM (ChAT 3.1); PB_2 VAChT 877 / ChAT 190; PB_3 VAChT 1,369 / ChAT 172.
* Wolff & Rubin 2018 (PMC6283239) Table 5 rows fetched verbatim: 'PB18.s-GxΔ7Gy.b | Delta7', 'PBG1-8.b-EBw.s-D/V GA.b | E-PG', 'PBG2-9.s-EBt.b-NO1.b | P-EN1, P-EN2', 'PBG2-9.s-FBℓ1.b-NO3P.b | P-FNP', 'PBG2-9.s-FBℓ2.b-NO3A.b | P-FNA', 'PBG2-9.s-FBℓ3.b-NO2D.b | P-FND'; Table 2 lists SS00090 for PBG1-8.b-EBw.s-D/V GA.b (E-PG) and SS00078 for PBG2-9.s-FBℓ3.b-NO2D.b (P-FND). The CX alias rows are sound.
* PAM aliases match supp. 1A compartments and the Aso 2014 / hemibrain numbering: PAM_1 = PAM-b'2a -> PAM02; PAM_2 = b'2a, g3, g4, g4<g1g2, g5 -> PAM02/12/08/07/01; PAM_3 = b1, b2 -> PAM04/09/10; PAM_4 = g4, g4<g1g2 -> PAM08/07; PAM_5 = g5 -> PAM01; PAM_6 = b'2m, g3, g4, g5 -> PAM06/12/08/01; PAM_7 = a1 -> PAM11; PAM_8 = g3 -> PAM12; PAM_9 = b1 -> PAM09/10; MBON_bp1 = MBON-b'1 -> MBON10; MBON_g1pedc -> MBON11. R8_Rh5 -> R8p, R8_Rh6 -> R8y, R7 (Rh3+Rh4, BL-8604) -> R7p/R7y/R7d, R1-6 -> R1-R6 (flywireType R1-6) are correct.
* Octalpha2R = CG18208 is correct (NCBI Gene 42258 aliases Dmel_CG18208; table 3 gene_id FBgn0038653). nAChRalpha1-7 / nAChRbeta1-3, ort, HisCl1, Oamb, Octbeta1-3R, GABA-B-R1/2/3, mGluR, mAChR-A/B/C, GluClalpha, GluRIA/IB, Nmdar1/2, Rdl/Lcch3/Grd, Dop1R1/Dop1R2/Dop2R/DopEcR, 5-HT1A/1B/2A/2B/7 and all 12 synthesis genes are present under those exact symbols in table 4 (no nAcRalpha-96Aa / HisCl2 aliases needed).

Every n_cells_malecns, out_syn_malecns and malecns_nt value matches cache/neurons.parquet + W_post_pre.npz (0 mismatches over 132 rows); class selectors ChAT 104,040 cells / 73,635,616 out-syn, Gad1 22,135 / 25,924,948, VGlut 29,616 / 21,179,300 confirmed. Optic-internal synapses 47,112,720, both ends matched 11,039,319 = 23.4 %. visual_centrifugal 563 cells: 14 cells (Lat1/2/5) matched, 0 out-syn. cb_intrinsic 32,160 cells, 37,466,904 out-syn: alias 15 types / 1,980 cells / 3.0 %, fuzzy 15 / 971 / 1.3 %, unmatched 90.8 % cells / 95.7 % out. L1.L2 pool: VGlut 929.6 / VAChT 868.4 / ChAT 241.2; pure L2: VAChT 1,296.7, ChAT 413.8, VGlut 6.6. HS/VS pool: VAChT 892.9, ChAT 65.0, VGlut 140.1, Gad1 75.0. Lat pool: ple 904, VGlut 193, VAChT 202, SerT 196, Gad1 121, Hdc 76.
* Tm5Y, Tm5a/b/c, TmY14, Mi19, T2, T3, LPi43 and all LC types except LC4/LC6/LC10a/LC10b/LC10d/LC16 are absent from every Davis cell-level column and from supp. 1A, so the plan's 'Davis 2020 Tm5Y glutamate' statement indeed has no source in this dataset.
* Tests: tests/test_receptor_model.py (the only test file touching these tables) passes locally, 8 passed in 10.1 s (CPU). Not applicable to this task key: there is no implementation diff or probe score to reproduce for tables:davis2020, so no cluster run was made (receptor_model byte-identity and slow-term sign belong to the implementation task, not this one).

## verify:tables:nern2025  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* Claim 8 / summary: 'in all 21 FlyWire misses the male-volume labels match the experiment' and 'FlyWire ... is wrong in every one of the 21 arbitrated cases'. Four of the 21 (Mi15 ACh vs ACh+Dop; R8p, R8y, HBeyelet ACh/Glu vs His+ACh) are the co-transmission rows where Nern and MaleCNS fail the same string comparison (they call ACh / His); FlyWire's ACh for Mi15 is as partially right as Nern's ACh. The male-volume labels beat FlyWire in 17 of 21, not 21 of 21.
* Doc section 7: 'Nern unclear types: 172 of 732 (3,258 MaleCNS cells, 2.6 % of OL-superclass output synapses)' is not reproducible and contradicts section 5 and claim 5. Recount: the 172 unclear exact types hold 3,593 MaleCNS cells (all superclasses) with 2,142,921 raw out syn (3.96 % of OL-superclass output); the 131 OL-superclass ones hold 3,452 cells / 1,888,216 syn (3.49 %). Section 5 / claim 5 are right; section 7 is wrong.
* Doc section 7: 'The MaleCNS annotation columns (flywireType / hemibrainType) ... agree with Sup_Table_7 where both exist'. Of 719 types with a Sup_Table_7 FlyWire name and a MaleCNS flywireType majority, 47 (6.5 %) agree with neither FlyWire column; there is a systematic off-by-one in the Sm numbering for Cm types (Cm20 Sm17 vs Sm18, Cm29 Sm37 vs Sm36, Cm30-35 likewise), shifted Li numbers (Li31/32/33/38/39), AOTU056 CB1558 vs CB2216, LoVP19 LTe49* vs LC46. The alias table carries both conflicting rows at tier 'alias' with no conflict flag.
* Doc section 3 arithmetic: '778 rows = 732 right-side types *_R + 97 left-side instances' is wrong (732 + 97 != 778); there are 681 _R rows + 97 _L rows, 732 distinct types. The script's stdout line 'Sup_Table_1: 732 right-side types' has the same mislabel.

### Corrections to apply
* Claim 9: Sup_Table_5 has 146 *rows* in the inventory but 145 distinct names (Tm29 appears twice); '146 in the inventory' should read 146 rows / 145 types; 145 map rows carry a label is correct.
* Claim 1 nuance ('0 name collisions', tier exact for all 732): the 48 central types are a name join onto cells MaleCNS has partly re-annotated since the optic-lobe release: AOTU056's figure bodyId 66210 is typed AOTU058 in MaleCNS v1.0 (AOTU056 5 cells R in Nern vs 3 R in MaleCNS; AOTU058 2 vs 4), and 19/681 right-side instances differ in cell count (AN09A005, LAL048, PVLP046, SMP217, SLP359, MeTu3a/b, ...). No NT consequence (all such types are 'unclear' or agree), but 'exact' for the central rows means same name, not verified same cells; the OL-superclass rows are verified (identical counts and figure bodyIds).
* Claim 2/3: the one 'central_or_vnc class' cell in the coverage table is an LC10_unclear cell whose superclass is visual_projection_tbc (and one ME_unclear cell is visual_projection_tbc), i.e. an OL cell excluded by the four-superclass filter, not a central cell.
* Claim 15: '59 ground-truth types' (paper, gt_count.csv sum 59) vs Sup_Table_5 Part_of_training_data = yes on 60 rows / 60 distinct names; the doc states both without reconciling them (the Methods count R8p+R8y as one type but those rows are training = no, so the 60-vs-59 gap is unexplained).
* Claim 4 wording: '43.8 % pre matched' is the raw-weight figure (0.4381); the |c.W| figure is 44.4 %. State which.
* Doc section 2: MOESM1 is 72 PDF pages (cover + 70-page catalogue), the doc says '70-page'.
* The alias table should either drop or flag the 47 types where Sup_Table_7's FlyWire name and MaleCNS flywireType conflict (especially the Cm -> Sm off-by-one series) before any downstream join uses tier 'alias' as trustworthy.

### Confirmed
* Files: all 20 files' SHA-256 and sizes recomputed and match docs/audits/receptor_sources_nern2025.md (MOESM1 64,469,458 B a142a82b..., MOESM4 zip 687,758 B ef34f64c..., Sup_Table_1 095a8014... identical to the GitHub params copy). Re-downloaded MOESM4 zip, GitHub SuppTable01 and gt_count.csv today: byte-identical.
* Sup_Table_1 independently re-read: 778 rows (681 _R + 97 _L instances), 732 distinct types, 46 bilateral types with L rows, 51 L-only types; per-type NT ACh 257 / Glu 158 / GABA 127 / His 8 / OA 6 / 5HT 2 / Dop 2 / unclear 172; 0 L/R contradictions; 0 duplicate names; 0 names with stray whitespace; 0 case collisions.
* All 732 Nern names occur verbatim in cache/neurons.parquet `type`. Same-volume evidence beyond the paper text: 777/778 'bodyId in figures' values exist in neurons.parquet with the identical type; 662/681 right-side instances have exactly the same cell count as MaleCNS somaSide=R cells of that type (photoreceptor differences are because R cells have no somaSide in MaleCNS).
* Type map: 784 rows = 732 exact + 13 class + 39 unmatched; exact set == Nern set; 684 exact types in OL superclasses (104,113 cells), 48 central (157 cells: cb_intrinsic 40, descending 6, ascending 2); n_cells_malecns and nern_nt columns match the parquet and Sup_Table_1 for every row (0 mismatches); out_syn_raw matches a recomputation from the raw weights feather for every row.
* Coverage recomputed from the parquet: OL superclasses 105,252 cells = exact 104,113 (98.918 %) + class 956 (0.908 %) + unmatched 145 (0.138 %) + untyped 38 (0.036 %); raw output synapses 54,130,575 with exact 53,911,934 + class 123,232 = 99.824 %; per superclass exact cells ol_intrinsic 99.86 %, visual_projection 99.42 %, visual_centrifugal 94.67 %, ol_sensory 84.73 % (+15.27 % class); whole CNS exact 62.40 % / class 0.57 % of cells.
* Edge-level recomputed: |c.W| OL-presynaptic both-matched 0.9199, pre-matched 0.9982, all both-matched 0.4073; raw 0.9193 / 0.9982 / 0.4015, raw all pre-matched 0.4381 (the reported 43.8 % is the raw figure; |c.W| gives 44.4 %).
* Cross-check tables reproduced exactly from the CSV: Nern vs MaleCNS 570 / 564 / 6 (428 cells, 253,093 syn: Tm31, Pm12, Li22, aMe17a, LC30, LoVP92); Nern vs FlyWire 544 / 503 / 41 (8,525 cells, 3,075,098 syn); Nern vs validation 140 / 136 / 4; MaleCNS vs validation 139 / 135 / 4 (Mi15, R8y, R8p, HBeyelet); FlyWire vs validation 139 / 118 / 21 (7,823 cells, 2,158,233 syn).

* T1: 1,777 cells, predicted_nt unclear 1,776 / histamine 1, celltype_predicted_nt histamine conf 0.514 for all, consensus_nt histamine, ground_truth empty, raw out 7,372; Sup_Table_5 row: TAPIN, 'unclear (not Glu,ACh,GABA,Dop,5HT,OA)', training no; Nern unclear; FlyWire ACh. TmY14: per-body Glu 209 / ACh 177 / unclear 91, type-level unclear 0.487, Nern Glu, FlyWire Glu, raw out 132,799. Mi19: 12/12 unclear (conf 0.41-0.49), Nern 5HT, EASI-FISH SerT, training yes, FlyWire dopamine. Tm5Y: ACh 898/898 conf 0.963, Nern ACh, FlyWire ACh, raw out 438,245, no Sup_Table_5 row.
* Unknown-NT rescue: 141 unknown cells in 16 matched types; Nern calls 6 types / 109 cells (TmY14 91, Mi19 12, aMe8 3, MeTu1 1, T2a 1, T5a_unclear 1).
* Class-tier rule verified against Sup_Table_1: all called subtypes agree for the 13 bins (LC10 5 ACh + LC10c-2 unclear; MeTu4a-f all ACh; T4a-d ACh; R7d/p/y and R8d/p/y His; ...), and the MaleCNS per-cell nt of every class bin equals the class label (T5a_unclear is 'unknown', the rescued cell). Disagreeing bins correctly left unmatched (Cm: GABA 20 / Glu 12 / ACh 6 / unclear 1; LPi: Glu 11 / GABA 4). The 39 unmatched OL types and their 145 cells match the parquet.
* Sup_Table_5: 148 rows, 147 distinct names (Tm29 twice: TAPIN + EASI-FISH), training yes 60 / no 88; NT ACh 60, Glu 37, GABA 29, OA 6, His 3, His+ACh 3, 5HT 2, Dop 2, ACh+Dop 1, unclear 5; methods EASI-FISH 62, TAPIN 57(+1 'RNASeq (TAPIN)'), antibody 13+2, FISH 9, FACS 2; [Cm11] and [Pm2] are the only names outside Sup_Table_1; 145 map rows carry a validation label, 60 in training / 85 not.

## verify:tables:central  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* T1 'ort 2.32 (Davie)' (claim 13; also the evidence string of type_map_central.csv line 556 and receptor_sources_central.md section 4 iii): expression_central.csv and a raw-MEX recompute give Davie T1 ort mean_log1p_cp10k 0.97, mean_cp10k 3.04, frac_expr 0.55 (428 cells); 2.32 matches no metric.
* FCA Mi15 'ple 0.66' (claims 10/11): expression_central.csv has ple 0.75 (pooled, 282 nuclei) and 0.63 (male, 114 nuclei); DAT 1.97 and VAChT 1.25 are correct.
* 'PAM ... 271,752 raw' output synapses (claim 10): 271,752 is PAM output to ALL postsynaptic bodies in the raw feather; the node-set-restricted base used everywhere else in the report (and in its own table 3d) gives 222,136 (|W| 0 is correct).
* '576 types matched (best tier: exact 37, alias 3, fuzzy 127, class 409); 86 unmatched source rows' (claim 4): recomputed from the shipped CSV, best tier per type is exact 36 / alias 3 / fuzzy 128 / class 409 (= the report's own 'All MaleCNS typed cells' table) and there are 87 unmatched rows (1,150 total - 1,063 matched); 576 distinct types is correct.
* 'Nothing in ... the CX columnar system (EPG/PEN/PFN/hDelta/FB), the DNs, the gustatory second-order cells ... is covered by either source' (COVERAGE prose): at class tier the NT-defined Serotonergic / Octopaminergic / Dopaminergic selections (NONVNC includes descending_neuron) pull in 13 DN types (23 cells: DNg26, DNg30, DNg34, DNg66, DNg104, DNge138, DNge149-152, DNp29, DNp32, DNpe048), 36 CX-class types (ER1-4 via Poxn, plus EL, ExR2, ExR3, FB1C, FB1H, FB2A, FB4L, FB4M, FB4Y, FB5H, PFGs, PFR_a) and 1 gustatory type (LB2b, 3 cells). EPG/PEN/PFN/hDelta are indeed uncovered.
* 'Transmitter cross-check ... 3 disagree' is incomplete: FCA 'transmedullary neuron Tm29' (exact tier, 115 nuclei) is VAChT 1.24 / frac 0.56, ChAT 0.47, VGlut 0.18 / frac 0.09 -- a cholinergic profile in the same range as FCA Tm20 / LC17 / Tm4 -- against MaleCNS Tm29 (= FlyWire Tm5d) glutamate 100 % (544 cells); the 2x/1.0 rule filed it as marker-silent ('-') only because mean(VAChT, ChAT) = 0.86 < 1.0. flyverse/data/type_aliases.csv line 20436 already records 'NT pred OL=glutamate FW=acetylcholine (MISMATCH)' for this type, so this is either a name collision (Ozel/FCA Tm29 != Nern Tm29) or a fourth disagreement; either way it should not be listed as agreement-neutral.
* 'the h5ad is 2,644,193,366 bytes (>2 GB, not downloaded)' (claim 1): no URL is recorded and the FCA share URL returns no Content-Length, so this figure could not be verified (treated as unconfirmed).

### Corrections to apply
* Fix the T1 evidence string in flyverse/data/type_map_central.csv (line 556) and receptor_sources_central.md section 4 (iii): 'ort 2.32' -> 'ort 0.97 mean log1p cp10k, 55 % of cells (mean cp10k 3.04)'.
* Claim 4 summary numbers should read: best tier exact 36 / alias 3 / fuzzy 128 / class 409 (576 types), 87 unmatched rows (1,150 - 1,063). The scratchpad script's summary json uses drop_duplicates without sorting by tier order, which is where 37/127 came from.
* Claim 10: PAM raw output should be 222,136 on the node-restricted base used throughout (271,752 is the unrestricted feather total); the same base confusion applies to any other 'raw' number quoted outside the tables (class DAN: 341,252 node-restricted vs 468,434 unrestricted).
* Claims 10/11: FCA Mi15 ple is 0.75 pooled / 0.63 male, not 0.66.
* The COVERAGE prose should say that class-tier NT-defined selections (Serotonergic / Octopaminergic / Dopaminergic, non-VNC incl. descending_neuron) do reach 13 DN types (23 cells), FB tangential / ExR / PFGs / PFR_a / EL CX types (94 non-ring CX cells) and LB2b (3 gustatory cells), and that these rows are circular for a transmitter cross-check and usable only as class-prior receptor profiles; 'nothing in EPG/PEN/PFN/hDelta' stays true.
* Add FCA Tm29 to the disagreement list (or mark the exact-name tier as suspect): FCA Tm29 VAChT 1.24 (56 %) / VGlut 0.18 (9 %) vs MaleCNS Tm29 (= FlyWire Tm5d) Glu 100 %; type_aliases.csv already flags Nern OL=Glu vs FlyWire=ACh for this type. The 'marker call' rule (score >= 1.0 and >= 2x runner-up on mean(VAChT, ChAT)) is too strict for FCA nuclear data, where confirmed cholinergic types sit at VAChT 1.2-1.6: nine FCA optic labels with MaleCNS ACh 100 % were filed as marker-silent for the same reason (L2, L3, L4, L5, Tm3a, Tm4, Tm20, TmY4, LC10/LC12/LC17, T2/T3/T4/T5), so the '40 marker-silent' count overstates silence; a per-source threshold (or frac_expr) would recover them.
* Record the h5ad URL alongside the size claim, or drop the size; and note that s-LNv is superclass visual_projection and l-LNv ol_intrinsic in MaleCNS, so only 7 of the 10 'Clock' types (44 of 52 cells) count toward the central-brain coverage tables (the tables themselves are already computed that way).
* Minor: the 'alias' tier for FCA 'transmedullary neuron Tm3a' -> Tm3 is a subset (Ozel's Tm3a is half of Tm3), not a synonym; and 'auditory sensory neuron' (706 nuclei, the broader FBbt term) maps to only 10 subclass-auditory JO types while the narrower 'Johnston organ neuron' (541) maps to all 34 JO-* types -- inverted specificity, harmless for coverage but worth a note.

### Confirmed
* Files and hashes: s_fca_biohub_head_10x.loom 742,982,593 B SHA-256 95de36ec...; Davie MEX tarball 219,256,938 B 5beaf137...; metadata tsv.gz bb0e13e2...; README 847d1f0b...; mmc2 0d4743d8..., mmc3 72cf6a8f..., mmc4 f8d78444..., mmc5 dfd68a86...; extracted matrix.mtx dc64ae27... (941,835,490 B), genes.tsv 24e7dd76..., barcodes.tsv d5bef617..., annotation.tsv 45c3f2e6...; tar listing confirms the four members. FCA share URL still serves s_fca_biohub_head_10x.loom (HTTP 200).
* FCA loom: /matrix 13,056 x 100,527 float32, integer-valued (sampled); 82 annotation labels; 'unannotated' 44,906 (44.7 %); sex male 47,409 / female 49,105 / mix 4,013; 54 of 55 genes present by modern symbol, only mAChR-C absent (no CG12796 alias either) -> 328 NaN cells = 82 labels x 2 metrics x 2 sex subsets, all in column mAChR-C.
* Davie MEX: header '17473 56902 70776454', integer counts (max 26,408), 41 numeric labels = 35,818 cells (62.9 %), 75 named labels = 21,084 cells; sex male 27,854 / female 29,048; ages {0,1,3,6,9,15,30,50}; genotypes DGRP-551 29,137 / w1118 27,765. All 55 genes present (KaiR1D as CG3822 FBgn0038837, Octalpha2R as CG18208 FBgn0038653, mAChR-A/B/C, nAChRalpha1-7/beta1-3, Octbeta1-3R, ort, Oamb by modern symbols). The case-colliding symbols Dat (FBgn0019643, N-acetyltransferase) / DAT (FBgn0034136, transporter), hdc/Hdc, trh/Trh were resolved correctly: the CSV carries the transporter/enzyme symbols (Davie G-KC DAT 0.023 vs Dat 0.385).
* expression_central.csv units and values: 560 rows (Davie 116 labels x 2 metrics; FCA 82 x 2 x {all, male}); recomputing mean log1p(cp10k) and frac_expr straight from the raw MEX for 21 clusters x 37 genes and from the raw loom for 8 labels x 20 genes x {all, male} reproduces every value to <= 0.0005 (rounding). All other quoted numbers check: TmY14 VGlut 4.04 / VAChT 0.23 (Davie, 1,399 cells) and 1.90 / 0.16 (FCA, 153 nuclei); T1 Hdc 0.00 both sources, VAChT 0.34 / 0.25, VGlut 0.33 / 0.10, Gad1 0.15 / 0.12, Octbeta2R 4.04, 5-HT1A 3.03, nAChRalpha7 3.19; LNv (41 cells) Pdf 6.29, SerT 0.00, Trh 0.00, Tbh 1.07; Pm3 (82 cells) VGlut 3.97 / Gad1 0.20, Pm1/Pm2/Pm3 Gad1 3.44; Poxn Gad1 3.20 (Davie) / 2.12 (FCA), Rdl 4.22, nAChRalpha6 2.70, nAChRalpha7 2.44, Octalpha2R 2.50, Oamb 1.24, GluClalpha 0.34, KaiR1D 0.09; G-KC Rdl 3.29, nAChRalpha6 3.26, Dop2R 2.70, Dop1R2 1.83, Dop1R1 1.53, GluClalpha 0.66, sNPF 3.57; A/B*-KC trio 1.90, DAT 1.52; PAM Dop2R 4.24, GluClalpha 1.66, DAT 4.12; Mi1 Octbeta1R 2.52, GluClalpha 1.30; Dopaminergic ple 3.35 / DAT 3.33; ITP cluster ITP 0.41 / 24 % vs Mip/ITP 2.63 / 78 %; FCA Mi15 DAT 1.97, VAChT 1.25; dorsal rim area VAChT 1.03 (2 nuclei).
* type_map_central.csv: 1,150 rows, 576 distinct matched types, every n_cells_malecns equals the cache/neurons.parquet count, every matched type exists in MaleCNS v1.0, every exact row's label token equals its type; alias rows are exactly Gr21a/63a -> ORN_V, lamina wide-field 1/2 -> Lawf1/2, Tm3a -> Tm3, outer photoreceptor -> R1-R6. Table S2 (mmc2.xlsx) confirms the cited cluster ids and methods (8 G-KC, 28 a'/b'-KC, 42 Dopaminergic with PAM Fer2(+)/Fer2(-) subclusters, 64 OA/TA with Octopaminergic/Tyraminergic, 80 Poxn, 73 Pm3 'literature markers/mapping', 11 TmY14 'mapping', 45 Tm1/TmY8, 61 dFB 'data generated in this study', 57 MBON, 36 Clock, 37 T1, 86 DN1, 27 OPN with the Li 2017 sub-labels, res-8 30 IPC, 118 DCN, 119 Capa, 129 Gr43a, 151 CCAP).

Central 6,925 types / 36,773 cells / 40,871,360 |W| / 42,943,403 raw: exact 1 / 16, alias 1 / 55, fuzzy 89 / 4,986 (13.6 %) / 3,520,654 (8.6 %) / 3,748,142 raw (8.7 %), class 393 / 4,116 (11.2 %) / 2,830,171 (6.9 %) / 4,174,512 raw (9.7 %), any 484 / 9,173 (24.9 %) / 6,374,691 (15.6 %) / 7,946,738 raw (18.5 %). ## verify:tables:typing  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* 'none 3,700 types / 22,442 cells (13.4%) / 22,003,398 raw synapses (17.7%)' for the whole CNS: every 'none' row in the report and in docs/audits/receptor_sources_typing.md omits the 2,605 untyped cells (961,116 raw synapses) although the denominators include them, so rows sum to 164,501 cells, not 167,106. Recomputed from cache/neurons.parquet + the raw weights feather + flyverse/data/type_aliases.csv: none = 25,047 cells (14.99%) / 22,964,514 raw (18.52%). The 3,700-type count is correct.
* 'Adding MANC: none 412 types / 2,347 cells (1.4%) / 1.9%': correct is 4,952 cells (2.96%) / 3,321,550 raw (2.68%). The exact+alias total 97.04% / 97.32% is right.
* 'Central (cb_intrinsic) none 367 types / 5.9% cells / 5.5% synapses': the 880 untyped cb_intrinsic cells are missing from the none row; correct is 2,763 cells (8.59%) / 2,770,300 raw (7.08%).
* COVERAGE statement 'class-only 3.7% / 2.8%; none 9.7% / 15.0%': class row is right (6,221 cells / 3,436,901 raw) but none is 18,826 cells (11.27%) / 19,527,613 raw (15.74%).
* 'Per system ... FlyWire none 13.8% / 18.1%; hemibrain none 77.4% / 65.7%': correct 15.36% / 18.92% and 78.97% / 66.45% (same omission). VNC 'Group vnc, system manc none 0.6% / 0.4%' is 1,095 cells (5.38%) / 349,599 raw (1.85%) once the 980 untyped VNC cells are counted.
* 'Schlegel 2024 Supplementary Data 1-5 from github.com/flyconnectome/flywire_annotations v3.0.0' and every evidence string 'Schlegel 2024 SD1 v3.0.0': the downloaded files are commit 8587524c1748ce5ef2080822a2fc890fc03bf597, which GitHub resolves to tag v3.1.0 (released 2026-07-21, 'Updated FlyWire annotations matching the release of version 1.0 of the MaleCNS dataset'); tag v3.0.0 is commit a92610ef (2025-10-09). The 8,840-type figure and the 490-synonym-type figure describe v3.1.0.

MANC 'exact' (3,433 types) is mancType == type from the MaleCNS column with no independent MANC type list.

## verify:derive  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* DATA ERROR (Davis 2020 gene alias): the 'KaiR1D' column of flyverse/data/expression_davis2020.csv is GEO gene CG8916 (FBgn0030707), which FlyBase lists as a GABA-gated chloride-channel subunit, NOT KaiR1D. FlyBase KaiR1D = CG3822 (FBgn0038837), which is the symbol the Ozel and Davie/FCA tables use (scripts/build_ozel2021_tables.py SOURCE_SYMBOL, expression_central.csv header) -- the three sources are inconsistent. Proof: derived KaiR1D_tpm equals the GSE116969_dataTable4 CG8916 row exactly (T1 2.88, L1 120.99, Tm9 19.40, Mi4 115.0, Dm10 190.5); the header of expression_davis2020.csv and docs/audits/receptor_sources_davis2020.md line 49 state 'KaiR1D is CG8916'. The real KaiR1D (CG3822) is on (P(on)>=0.5 & TPM>=10) in 65 of 69 neuronal QC-pass Davis types (T1 94.4, L1 45.6, Tm9 107.3, Mi4 76.2, Mi1 45.9, Tm4 89.9, Dm10 75.3, Mi15 70.7, Dm9 71.4 TPM; off only in R1-6/R7/R8). Rebuilding in memory with CG3822 (nothing written): class-rule fast_net changes for 9 Davis-profiled rows (Mi15 +1->mixed, Lawf2 mixed->+1, Dm8a/Dm8b/LC10a/LLPC1/PAM07/PAM08 -1->mixed, <nt=acetylcholine> mixed->-1); nonmda net for 22 rows (C2/Dm11/Lawf2 -> +1; Dm1, Mi9, Tm2, TmY3, TmY5a, PFNp_a-e, Dm8a/b, LLPC1, PAM07/08 -> mixed; T4a-d mixed -> -1); abs net for 3 (KCg-d, Lawf2 -> mixed; Mi15 -> -1). Flip totals: class 902,325 -> 877,917 syn (4.3 -> 4.1 %), abs unchanged 127,309, nonmda 1,613,925 -> 1,703,230 (7.6 -> 8.0 %). Every lead-gene attribution involving KaiR1D/GluRIA in Davis types is wrong: corrected leads T1 KaiR1D (not GluRIA), L1 KaiR1D 45.6 TPM (not 121), Tm9 KaiR1D 107 (not 19.4), Tm4 KaiR1D 104 summed (not 146), Dm10/Mi15 nonmda lead GluRIB (not KaiR1D); by-lead-gene shares: class KaiR1D 10.0 % (reported 2.8 %), GluRIA 13.5 % (20.0 %), Nmdar2 52.5 %; abs KaiR1D 69.0 % (reported 19.9 %), GluRIA 0 (reported 49.0 %); nonmda GluRIB 51.8 / GluRIA 28.0 / KaiR1D 20.2 %.
* 'Nmdar2 is 95-727 TPM (P(on) >= 0.5 in 87 %) in every Davis type': false as stated. QC-pass neuronal Davis rows: Nmdar2 5.32-1,073 TPM; photoreceptors R8_Rh5 5.32, R8_Rh6 7.98, R7 30.6, R1-6 35.4 with P(on) = 0. Excluding photoreceptors the range is 102-1,073 with P(on) = 1 in 100 %. The '87 %' is 69 of 79 QC-pass rows including glia and muscle; among neuronal QC-pass rows it is 94 %.
* 'VGAT 51-311 TPM with P(on) = 1 in every Davis type incl. T1/L1/Mi1': range over QC-pass neuronal rows is 1.74-586 TPM; photoreceptors 1.7-14.5 with P(on) = 0; T1 172.6 / L1 304.6 / Mi1 236.1. The conclusion (VGAT does not discriminate among non-photoreceptor neurons: 60-586 TPM, P(on) = 1 in all) holds, the quoted range does not.
* 'Tdc2 is 4-15 TPM with P(on) 0.89': QC-pass neuronal range 0.64-35.1 TPM; P(on) mean 0.88, >= 0.5 in 91 %, = 1 in 39 %; the script's own 'on' rule (P(on) >= 0.5 AND TPM >= 10) fires in only 32 % of rows.
* 'Outside mixed pools 388 types are comparable with the model: 379 agree (97.7 %), 9 disagree': the 388 = 466 - 78 removes only the mixed-pool types that DISAGREE; 92 mixed-pool types whose pool call happens to match the model stay in the numerator (nt_by_type_transcriptome.csv: pool_mixed = True on 170 rows, 92 agree_model True, 78 False). Truly outside mixed pools: 296 comparable, 287 agree (97.0 %), 9 disagree.
* 'Nern 2025 label available for 384 of them' (COVERAGE): nern2025_prediction is non-empty for 101 of the 479 types (88 not 'unclear'); 384 = 297 + 82 + 5 = the types whose transcriptome call is comparable with the model, not Nern coverage.
* Claim 14 tallies over the 611 receptor rows are wrong for glutamate and the monoamines (recounted from the shipped receptors_by_type.csv): glutamate class rule +1 195 / -1 146 / mixed 174 / none 96 (reported 188/135/192/96); absolute rule +1 40 / -1 188 / mixed 287 / none 96 (reported 173/79/263/96); dopamine +402 / -132 / mixed 77 (reported -96 / 113); octopamine +194 / -32 / mixed 243 / none 142 (reported +221 / -25 / 223); serotonin +267 / -255 / mixed 44 / none 45 (reported 270/254/42). The acetylcholine (+301/-83/38/189), GABA-B (-603), mGluR (-415, 196 none) and source-mix tallies are correct.
* l-LNv 'SerT / Trh 0 in both sources': Davis lLNv (suboptimal-only driver) has SerT 5.4 TPM, Trh 0.0 (below the 10 TPM floor, so 'off', but not 0); Davie LNv SerT and Trh fraction 0.0.

### Corrections to apply
* Rebuild expression_davis2020.csv with KaiR1D := CG3822 (FBgn0038837; TPM from dataTable4, P(on) from dataTable7b, table-3 sample means for suboptimal drivers) and rerun scripts/build_receptor_table.py; fix the header sentence 'source symbols differ for KaiR1D (= CG8916)' and docs/audits/receptor_sources_davis2020.md line 49. Expected after the fix (from the in-memory corrected rebuild): class-rule glutamate flips 239,429 edges / 877,917 syn (4.1 %), lead genes Nmdar2 52.5 % / GluRIB 24.0 % / GluRIA 13.5 % / KaiR1D 10.0 %, Lawf2 enters the top list (28,751 syn) and Mi15 (53,159) leaves it; nonmda 370,422 / 1,703,230 (8.0 %), C2 enters (57,429); abs totals unchanged (127,309) but T1/Tm9/L1 are KaiR1D-led (69 % of abs flips), not GluRIA. Net-call changes for 9 (class), 22 (nonmda) and 3 (abs) Davis-profiled types are listed under refuted; the shipped fast_pos_lead_nonmda 'KaiR1D' count among Davis types goes 15 -> 50.

The '87 %' and '85 %' figures use the 79 QC-pass rows including glia and muscle as denominator -- say so.

## verify:implement  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* Claim 10 (reference baseline): the 'off' baseline quoted from out/bitter_test.txt (calibrated MN9 sugar 3.5 / sugar+bitter 0.0 / bitter 0.0 Hz; Shiu 97.6 / 1.1 / 0.0) is NOT what the current code gives. Rerun on the cluster (job skep-bitter-22e1fb, 4 jobs, 0 failed, 2.1 min): --receptor-model off twice -> calibrated 4.6 / 0.0 / 0.0 Hz, Shiu 123.5 / 2.1 / 0.0 Hz, byte-for-byte identical between the two runs and identical to out/bitter_off_r2.txt (18:22). Cause: bitter_test.txt was run at 16:00; commit 069deb0 (16:49:28, 'Session-9 audit workflow ... three bugs fixed') changed flyverse/brain.py afterwards (e.g. DEFAULT_STD_U_BY_TYPE PN entry removed, _shaped_weights copy); the receptor implementation itself is byte-identical to HEAD (see confirmed). The 'pending' part of claim 10 is also outdated: out/bitter-full.txt (18:09), bitter_sign.txt, bitter_signgain.txt, bitter_full.txt (18:28) exist and match my rerun exactly: sign -> calibrated 6.9 / 0.0 / 0.0, Shiu 5.6 / 0.9 / 0.0 (657 spikes/step); full -> calibrated 5.8 / 0.0 / 0.0, Shiu 74.2 / 1.8 / 0.0 (440 spikes/step); sign+gain (their run) -> 5.9 / 0.0 / 0.0, Shiu 122.5 / 2.2 / 0.0.
* Claim 5, causal wording 'the profiled type has no ort/HisCl1 call' for Tm5a / Tm5b (14,953 R7 synapses silenced): the 'profile' is the davie2018 single-nucleus cluster 'Tm5ab' (Table S2 res2 cluster 41, tier fuzzy, list rule Tm5a|Tm5b, 1,146 cells pooled) with HisCl:off and every fast_pos/neg_val 0.000, while alt_sources for the same rows say ozel2021(class): fast=-1 (ort on) and Tm5c (ozel2021 exact) keeps ort. Tm5a/b/c are the canonical ort-expressing R7 targets (Gao 2008; Karuppudurai 2014), and docs/audits/receptor_rules.md line 523 itself says the single-nucleus sources' 'none' classes are less trustworthy; the fuzzy>class tier ordering picks the dropout-prone profile. So these silencings are a tier-ordering artefact, not a receptor finding. Same mechanism for the 150,000 glutamate/GABA synapses silenced ONTO photoreceptors (silenced top post types R7_unclear 32,122, R7y 27,614, R8_unclear 25,867, R8y 25,792, R7p 20,233, R8p 19,486): the fca2022 'photoreceptor cell R7/R8' profiles (404 / 442 nuclei) have GluCl:off, iGluR:off, mGluR:off, HisCl:off.

### Corrections to apply
* Claim 4/5 denominator: '687,278 photoreceptor synapses' is the |W| total of ALL histamine-labelled presynaptic cells; the PHOTORECEPTOR_TYPES carry 647,332 |W| syn (581,246 onto matched targets). The silenced 116,366 are all photoreceptor-pre (R8_unclear 31,016, R8y 26,844, R8p 19,527, R7_unclear 10,988, R7y 10,440, R7p 6,370, R1-R6 3,234, R7d 3,111), i.e. 18.0 % of photoreceptor output, 20.0 % of photoreceptor output onto profiled targets.
* The default net_rule='class' makes the glutamate flips rest on the table's own NMDA caveat: 485,355 of the 902,325 flipped synapses (53.8 %) have Nmdar2 as the lead + gene (Mi4 787.5 vs GluClalpha 820.7 TPM; Mi9 879.4 vs 1,729.3; Mi15 656.5 vs 1,149.1; Dm10 1,272.8 vs 953.3), and 533,112 (59.1 %) land on types whose GluClalpha absolute level exceeds the iGluR level; 691,738 (76.7 %) of the flipped synapses come from davis2020 profiles. Under net_rule='abs' only 45,872 entries / 127,309 syn flip (top T1 62,360, Dm9 25,467, Tm9 22,796); under 'nonmda' 355,958 / 1,613,925 (Mi1 254,126, Tm4 197,167 added). The 40,144 silenced entries are identical under all three rules. The report's sign-change claims are exact for 'class' but the choice of rule moves the flipped mass by 13x.
* Slow-term magnitude ('full'): per synapse-equivalent the slow jump 0.0275 mV with tau 200 ms integrates to 5.5 mV*ms per spike vs 0.275 mV x 5 ms = 1.375 mV*ms for a fast synapse (4.0x per synapse); a capped 60-synapse edge at 50 Hz holds g_slow at 16.5 mV steady state vs 4.1 mV for the fast g, against a 7 mV rest-to-threshold gap. In the GPU subset run |g_slow| summed to 467,411 mV over 8,288 neurons after 300 ms (6,884 neurons negative, 938 positive; 448,924 mV on the clocked path). 98.4 % of the slow entries sit on ACh / GABA / glutamate edges (mAChR-B, GABA-B, mGluR); the monoamines the plan targeted are 85,293 entries (1.6 %). slow_gain=0.1 therefore is not a small perturbation; it is a parameter still to sweep (the report says so only in passing).
* Under the Shiu uniform-weight setting the 'sign' model breaks the sugar response: MN9 sugar 123.5 Hz (off) -> 5.6 Hz with 657 spikes/step (5.8x the off network activity of 114); 'full' 74.2 Hz (440 spikes/step); 'sign+gain' 122.5 Hz. The calibrated setting (fan-in normalisation, cap, path gains) masks this: 4.6 (off) / 6.9 (sign) / 5.9 (sign+gain) / 5.8 (full) Hz, sugar+bitter and bitter 0.0 throughout, so the sugar/bitter replication holds only in the calibrated regime.
* Claim 6 wording 'synapse-equivalents ... before scaling': the quoted 23,596,368 (+9,278,324 / -14,318,042) and the per-NT / per-module sums are AFTER the gain-class factors (low 0.5 / high 1.5) and the 60-per-edge cap; raw count x slow sign is 22,135,995 (+9,339,920 / -12,796,075), with gain factors but no cap 24,348,424; 23,596,368 x 0.0275 = 648,900 mV, the 618,532.8 mV figure is after the fan-in scale.
* Claim 7 count sum: 124,161,873 (float64), the reported 124,161,872 is float32 rounding; claim 8 sums differ by 1 mV from mine for the same reason (29,199,737 vs 29,199,736 etc.).
* scripts/cluster_run.py --fetch keeps only the last flag (my batch fetched only out/skep_bitter_full.txt); the other three probe outputs are in the job log (scratchpad/cluster_bitter.log). My verification artefacts: scratchpad/verify_lookup.py/.out, verify_bytes.py/.out, verify_optic.py/.out, skep_gpu_full.py (moved out of scripts/ after the run), out/skep_gpu_full.txt, out/skep_bitter_full.txt. No file of the implementer was edited; HEAD moved to da20008 during this check (manifest commits by another agent), model code unchanged since 069deb0.

## verify:score  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Corrections to apply
* Claim 7 (LC10a per-cell drive): the 'sign' range is wider than reported. A fourth replicate on the cluster (out/sk_fg_sign.txt) gives LC10a (apple - none) per-cell max +0.14 mV (report: 0.23 / 0.29 / 0.27), LC10b 90th percentile +0.44 mV (report 0.63 / 0.63 / 0.62), LPLC2 population 1.73 / 1.49 (matches). Off rerun: LC10a max +0.09, LC10b 90th +0.05, LPLC2 1.52 / 1.40. So the 'grows 2-4x' statement should read 'LC10a best cell 0.07-0.13 -> 0.14-0.29 mV (1.1-4x), LC10b 90th percentile 0.05-0.14 -> 0.44-0.63 mV'; the conclusion (25-50x short of the 7 mV threshold, rate 0.02 Hz) is unaffected.
* Claim 2 wording 'flipped Glu -1 -> +1 ... onto iGluR-dominant targets' (report section 1) is a tertile-rank artefact, not an expression fact: in the Davis 2020 profiles that decide the four largest targets, GluClalpha TPM >= the whole iGluR sum on Mi4 (820.7 vs 787.5, the + side led by Nmdar2), Mi9 (1729.3 vs 879.4), L3 (577.5 vs 339.7) and T2a (Özel, 5.3 vs 3.9 in expm1 units). Under the same table's 'abs' rule only 45,872 entries / 127,309 syn flip (13.5 % of the class-rule flipped synapses; Mi4, L3, T2a, Mi9, Dm10, Mi15 all 0), under 'nonmda' 355,958 / 1,613,925 (Mi9 drops out). The Mi9, L3 and Tm9 flips (245,248 syn = 27 % of all flips) are contradicted by every alternative source in the table's alt_sources column (ozel2021 / fca2022 / davie2018 all fast=-1); Mi4 is supported by ozel2021 (+1) and T2a's own source is ozel2021. The verdict that 'sign' is 'the only option worth keeping' therefore rests on 531,322 flipped synapses (59 % of the 902,325) that the abs rule would not make -- the report says abs was not scored, so this should be stated next to the coverage caveat rather than as 'iGluR-dominant targets'.
* Report section 1 'before gain classes +9.28 M / -14.32 M' slow synapse-equivalents is not reproduced: count x slow_sign gives +9,339,920 / -12,796,075 uncapped and +9,001,051 / -12,418,911 capped at 60; only the with-gain figures (+9,548,145 / -14,800,280, the ones in key claim 11) match. The '23.6 M' total should be 22.1 M (uncapped) or read 'with gain classes 24.3 M'.
* 'Eager off control ... the backend change alone changes nothing' is a control for the demo sections only: rest, taste, smell, dn, walk, walk.power, bitter and their spikes/step are bit-identical between out/rm_off.json and out/rm_off_eager.json (e.g. taste 5.845469, smell PN 12.386305, KC_active 1599, walk.GF_max 8.51), i.e. the Brain-only sections run the same Torch path in both 'native' and 'eager' suites; only loom_escape / walk_gf / rotation / object / wind / odour differ. The statement is true but its evidence covers 15 of 29 checks.
* Claim 8 'walking spikes/step 1-11 -> 19-70': the off logs show 1, 11 and 51 spikes/step at the three walking reports (sign: 70, 19, 23); the report's 'first second' qualifier excludes the 51, so the comparison is 1-11 vs 19-70 only for the first two reports and 51 vs 23 for the third -- the walking activity rise under 'sign' is front-loaded, not uniform.
* Section 4 / caveats describe the figure-ground and rotation replicates as sampling 'the native backend's run-to-run nondeterminism': confirmed to be the right scatter model -- my independent cluster replicate of every probe fell inside the reported ranges (loom 24 vs 21-24 off; Mi4 z 2.5 vs 2.1-3.9; DNp20 -15.3 vs -11.1 to -13.7; DNp04 d' 0.46 vs 0.1-0.6) except the LC10a / LC10b per-cell drive noted above.

### Assessment

Overall: the pipeline is built and audited end-to-end, the scientific result is null (no mode improves the suite without breaking bitter.shiu_sugar), and three things limit what the round can claim. (i) Coverage is 25.7 % of |W| synapses under the table (exact 14.8 %, fuzzy 7.0 %, class 2.7 %, alias 1.2 %; optic module 52.2 %, visual_projection 37.6 %, central 4.8 %, VNC 0) and every named target of hypotheses (a)-(c) except LC10a/LPLC2/LPi34/HSN/HSE is in the uncovered 74 %. (ii) The only positive 'sign' effects (Mi4 figure z +0.0 -> +3.1; pinned loom GF 21-24 -> 32 Hz; demo escapes 3/3) rest on class-rule glutamate flips of which 531,322 of 902,325 syn (59 %) are not made by the abs rule (127,309 syn) and 245,248 syn (Mi9, L3, Tm9) are contradicted by every alternative source in the table; the abs / nonmda rules were never scored. (iii) Data errors remain in the shipped tables (KaiR1D = CG8916; Pm1 exact; single-nucleus 'none' classes silencing 286,600 syn).

COVERAGE GAP THAT MATTERS MOST -- small-object pathway: the receptor route cannot reach it with the present sources. Tm5Y (898 cells, 406,046 |W| input syn, 25.1 % glutamatergic: Tm32 15,144, Dm8a 10,487, Tm37 8,976, Li16 7,438, Dm3b 5,774), TmY21 (372, 201,182, 30.2 % Glu: TmY5a 16,818, Dm3a 5,743, LC20b 5,388, LT88 5,248), TmY13 (432, 241,181, 30.3 % Glu, Mi9 alone 60,503), LC11 (143, 342,940, 12.4 % Glu), Y3 (627, 805,508, 24.7 % Glu), Li19, Tm32 have 0 % profiled input in all four expression sources (Nern 2025 gives NT only: all ACh). The ChAT-class fallback would give them GluCl-dominant profiles (no change). So the sign-flippable mass in that stage (Tm5Y 102 k, TmY21 61 k, TmY13 73 k, Y3 199 k glutamatergic syn) is decided by NT_SIGN and will stay so until a Tm5Y/TmY21/TmY13/LC11/Y3 profile is found (candidate: Kurmangaliyev et al. 2020 GSE156455 adult optic lobe, or Ozel's 111 unannotated clusters keyed by Nern 2025 marker genes -- unverified, to check). Under 'sign' the object-detector drive grows only LC10a best cell 0.07-0.13 -> 0.14-0.29 mV (7 mV threshold), rate 0.02 Hz either way; and the decisive test (moving 1 cm ball, 12 cm in 3 s, LC11/LC10 vs LPLC2) was never run with the receptor model because it has no script. The medulla 'sign' figure (Mi4 36 % of input flipped via TmY16 / Dm4 / Mi9 / Dm6 / Dm9) is where the contested flips live.

Compass: not a receptor-coverage problem on the ring itself. CX: 2,945 cells / 290 types / 3,041,493 |W| input syn, 33.6 % onto profiled targets; pre composition ACh 44.5 %, Glu 36.3 %, GABA 19.1 %, monoamine 0 in |W|. EPG (alias, Davis PB_2 = clean E-PG driver SS00090): glutamate GluClalpha 10,283 vs iGluR 911 TPM (Nmdar2-led) -> fast -1 under abs / nonmda, 'mixed' -> NT_SIGN prior -1 under class, slow none; so the ExR6 6,236 / ExR5 6,057 / Delta7 4,294 / ExR4 1,660 syn onto EPG (9.8 % of its input) are fast GluCl inhibition by the data -- the cx_wedge 'untuned global loop' is confirmed as fast inhibitory, and the receptor model changes 0 EPG / PEN / Delta7 entries (Delta7 -> EPG x15-25 remains a dynamics / gain question). PEN_a/b glutamate 27-33 % of input (ExR6 5,089, ExR4 2,092 / Delta7 6,061, ExR4 5,030) is profiled only through Davis PB_3 (GluCl 4,517 vs 98; mGluR slow -), a driver Davis flags 'many OL cells including Mi1; also GF; OL cells outnumber cells in PB'. The real compass gaps: GLNO -> PEN (16,371 raw syn = 19.4 % of PEN input, sign 0, GLNO in no source, T-bars Glu 51 % / ACh 37 %); the unprofiled output stage PFL3 (57,774 |W| in-syn, 47 % Glu), hDeltaA-M (24-43 k each), FC2A-C, PFL2, ExR1 (51,286), PEG, ER6, ExR4/ExR6, EPGt, hDeltaK (ACh vs serotonin unresolved) -- 66 % of CX input; and monoamine inputs (ExR2 DA, ExR3 5-HT, FB4Y, EL OA) that only a working slow term could sign.

LEAST TRUSTWORTHY MAPPING (ranked by synapses it decides): (1) the tier rule 'fuzzy/exact single-nucleus profile beats a class profile with the receptor on' -> 40,144 silenced entries / 286,600 syn: fca2022 'photoreceptor cell R7/R8' class rows (GluCl/iGluR/HisCl all off) silence ~150 k Glu/GABA syn onto R7_unclear 32,122, R7y 27,614, R8_unclear 25,867, R8y 25,792, R7p 20,233, R8p 19,486; Davie 'Tm5ab' pool (1,146 cells, HisCl off) silences 14,953 R7 -> Tm5a/b syn although ozel2021 (class) has ort on and Tm5a/b are canonical ort targets; Mi1 loses 13,013 R8 histamine syn and its ON figure (z -8.9 -> -1.2). Dropout artefacts on the object pathway's input. (2) Davis PB_3 -> PEN_a/PEN_b (the only PEN profile, Mi1-contaminated). (3) KaiR1D = CG8916 (Davis, SOURCE_PRIORITY 0): 102 glutamate rows, wrong nonmda lead on Dm10, Dm4, L1, L2, Mi15, Mi9, T4a-d, Tm2, Tm20, Tm3, Tm4, Tm9. (4) 78 mixed-pool class priors (FCA 'antennal lobe projection neuron' 180 types incl. 209 GABAergic vPN cells called ACh; Davie 'MBON' 37 types; 17 % of AL edges) and the circular NT-class selectors (Dopaminergic / Serotonergic / Octopaminergic -> 13 DN types, FB tangential, ExR2/3, EL). (5) typing alias tier: 903 of 4,589 FlyWire alias rows name types absent from SD1 v3.1.0 (448 types, 1,834,535 raw syn), 47 Cm->Sm conflicts; Pm1 exact (3-way MaleCNS split); Tm29 exact vs FCA Tm29 cholinergic.

SLOW TERM -- not the right mechanism as built. Evidence: (a) it is a classical-metabotropic term, not a monoamine one: 5,238,228 entries, 98.4 % on ACh / GABA / Glu edges (mAChR-A/B, GABA-B, mGluR: ACh 3.17 M, GABA 1.42 M, Glu 0.56 M syn-eq vs DA 30,771, OA 36,045, 5-HT 18,477); the plan's target -- signed DA / OA / 5-HT -- gets a non-zero slow sign on 225,430 of 1.85 M monoamine syn (12.2 %; class rule; 270,344 KC dopamine syn 'mixed' by DopEcR-vs-Dop2R ties), 26.9 % under abs. (b) The dopamine + sign is DopEcR-led in 560 of 607 types (Dop1R1 38), i.e. rests on an ecdysone receptor with micromolar DA affinity. (c) Magnitude: 0.0275 mV x tau 200 ms = 5.5 mV.ms per synapse vs 1.375 for a fast one (4x); a capped edge at 50 Hz holds g_slow 16.5 mV against a 7 mV rest-threshold gap; one tau for all classes -> KC 37 Hz, MBON/DPM 330 Hz, 31 hops / 15 s, loom GF 0, wind DNp18 45 -> 4.6. (d) Form: an additive membrane conductance driven spike-by-spike; in the animal GABA-B / mAChR-B act mostly presynaptically (release, adaptation), KC Dop1R1 gates plasticity (cAMP/PKA), OA / 5-HT on visual neurons scale gain -- multiplicative tone, not a current; and the rate optic lobe, where the OA centrifugal output (17.6 % silenced) lands and where 47 % of OA syn onto profiled targets sit, has no slow term at all. (e) It forces the Torch path (no native kernel), 13.3 vs 9.6 min per suite. Conclusion: keep the sparse W_slow machinery, but split classical-metabotropic from monoamine classes with separate scales (classical default 0 or recast as a presynaptic depression / adaptation modifier), make the monoamine term a slow multiplicative gain or threshold offset on the target with per-class tau, add it to optic.py, and sweep 0.01-0.03 x w_syn on rest / smell.KC / walk_gf / loom_escape before assay 7 is attempted.

Expected from the structure: the receptor model changes 0 EPG / PEN / Delta7 entries (EPG glutamate GluClalpha 10,283 vs iGluR 911 TPM; PEN 4,517 vs 98), so cx_sign should reproduce cx_wedge.md's bump; the GLNO runs decide whether a silent PEN<->GLNO loop matters for persistence. (2) Record in receptor_integration.md that the ExR4/ExR6/ExR5/Delta7 -> EPG glutamate (19,000 syn, 9.8 % of EPG input) is fast GluCl inhibition by the E-PG driver data and that PEN's only profile is the Mi1-contaminated Davis PB_3 driver; the compass ring's open items (bump under sensory input, PEN L/R shift, PFN -> hDelta -> PFL3 where PFL3 57,774 / hDelta 24-43 k in-syn are unprofiled) are dynamics experiments, not receptor ones.
5. SLOW TERM REDESIGN AND SWEEP (needs code in flyverse/brain.py + flags in scripts/benchmark.py, then one cluster batch): add LIFParams.slow_gain_by_class = {'metabotropic_classical': 0.0, 'monoamine': g} and slow_tau_by_class (mAChR/GABA-B/mGluR vs DA/OA/5-HT; the plan asked for per-class time constants), and a multiplicative variant (g_slow scales the fast input or shifts the threshold rather than adding to the membrane target); add the same term to optic.OpticLobe (the OA visual-centrifugal output, 17.6 % silenced, lands in the rate model). Then: python scripts/cluster_run.py --name slow-sweep --minutes 60 with benchmark.py --eager --receptor-model full --slow-gain 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.03 --slow-classical 0 (one job each) scored on rest.spikes_per_step, smell.KC_hz (< 5), walk_gf.p99 (< 38), loom_escape.GF_peak (>= 33), wind.DNp18. Only after a setting passes those four is assay 7 (hunger) worth an experiment: starvation -> DAN / OA tone hook in body.Metabolism -> KC / GNG targets (Dop1R1 leads the + group in 38 types only; DopEcR in 560 -- report the sign under Dop1R1-only as a variant).
6. APPLY THE DERIVED TRANSMITTER LABELS (step 3, second half; CPU + one suite run): TmY14 -> glutamate (91 unknown cells, 26,208 raw out syn; Davie / FCA / Ozel + Nern agree), Mi19 -> serotonin (12 cells, 24,996 syn; EASI-FISH validated; sign 0 until the slow term works), aMe8 -> acetylcholine (4 / 7,238), T1 -> unknown (1,777 cells, 7,372 out syn; histamine conf 0.51 unsupported by Davis / Ozel / Davie / FCA, Nern unclear), as a TYPE_NT_OVERRIDE table in flyverse/connectome.py with the source in a comment; decide on the NT audit's type-majority rule for the 496 of 1,838 unknown presynaptic cells (25 % of silenced synapses). Verify: PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8 python scripts/audit_nt.py (sign-0 share 2.2 % -> expected ~2.1 %), then one cluster benchmark run (python scripts/cluster_run.py --name nt-override --minutes 15 "python scripts/benchmark.py --seeds 0,1,2 > out/rm_ntov.txt; cat out/rm_ntov.txt").
7. STEP 8, STOP-GAP RETIREMENT WITH THE RECEPTOR MODEL ON: add --receptor-model / --receptor-net-rule to scripts/retire_measures.py and rerun the three named measures under sign (class and abs): optic.DEFAULT_PAIR_GAIN LPi34/43 -> LPLC2 x4 (LPi34 and LPLC2 are profiled, their glutamate stays GluCl -1: the data do not reproduce the x4, so it stays a stop-gap unless the loom / walk_gf checks pass at x1 under sign), the GF x0.3 input damping (anti_runaway.md: removable; four of five targets inhibitory) and UNKNOWN_NT_OVERRIDE_REGEX (27 AL LNs, 136,223 syn). python scripts/cluster_run.py --name retire-rm --minutes 60 "python scripts/retire_measures.py --receptor-model sign --measures pair_gain_lpi,gf_damp,al_ln_override --replicates 3 > out/retire_rm.txt; cat out/retire_rm.txt". Adopt one at a time with a suite run each, as anti_runaway.md prescribes.
8. CLOSE THE REPRODUCIBILITY AND REPORTING GAPS (CPU): (a) docs/audits/receptor_sources.md as the index the plan named, pointing at the five per-source files, with one combined coverage table (per superclass x tier x source) and the 'share of synapses under which rule' statement for every module; (b) propagate the skeptic corrections into receptor_sources_*.md, receptor_rules.md (Nmdar2 5.3-1,073 TPM incl. photoreceptors off; VGAT 1.7-586; Tdc2 on in 32 % by the script's own rule; agreement 296 / 287 outside mixed pools; Nern label for 101 of 479) and receptor_integration.md (LC10a best-cell range 0.14-0.29 mV; slow syn-eq 22.1 M uncapped / 24.3 M with gains; 'iGluR-dominant targets' -> 'iGluR tertile above GluCl tertile'); (c) add --seed to probe_figure_ground.py and run 3 Poisson seeds of probe_bitter.py per mode in the next batch (currently 1); (d) NOTES session-10 entry and a commit of the 9 modified + 29 untracked files once (a)-(b) are in; (e) infrastructure: cluster_run --fetch keeps only the last path (fetch a directory or accept repeated flags) and two batch-1 jobs on GPU 2 started with torch.cuda.is_available() == False -- check the node agent's CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES when several budgeted jobs start in the same second.

# Round 2: adversarial verification record

Round 2 (workflow `wf_6321ee2a-6e5`: five table fixes + profile search, rebuild, four scoring tasks, six skeptics (Opus),
one critic) re-checked every deliverable. Verdicts verbatim. Corrections marked in the per-document 'Corrections
(round-2 verification)' sections were applied to the docs; the code defects (warp/torch downgrade, gain-mode wording,
cx_wedge --out guard, cluster_run directory fetch, scratchpad generators) were fixed in the same commit. Numbers not
yet re-measured (flagged 'open' below) are carried into round 3.
