The new drain-1.0 / 5-min cell at brain seed 2 (env 32-47) REVERSES: off mean 6.557 cm / median 7.317 vs default 8.401 / 8.325 (U 145, p 0.534; out/skfeed_default_d1.0_5min_s2.json, out/skfeed_off_d1.0_5min_s2.json). The new drain-0.5 / 5-min cell at brain seed 1 (env 16-31) is null with off's median LOWER: default 3.148 / 2.517 vs off 3.552 / 2.308 (U 130, p 0.955; out/skfeed_default_d0.5_5min_s1.json, out/skfeed_off_d0.5_5min_s1.json). Run-level over all six pairs: default closer in 5/6, exact sign test p 0.2188, Wilcoxon p 0.1562, paired t p 0.1068. The '3 of 3 cells' was a four-run streak that breaks at six runs.
* THE STATED CAUSE OF THE NEGATIVE min_distance TAIL IS GEOMETRICALLY IMPOSSIBLE. Sections 5.1 item 2 and 5.3 item 4 both say the closest-approach distribution 'runs continuously from -2.1 cm (the fly walks through the apple's footprint: there is no sphere collision for a walking fly, so |p - centre| - radius goes negative)'. With --fence, flyverse/batch_body.py:145-157 updates only x, y and heading, so a walking fly sits at exactly z = table_top = 0.75 (all 416 re-read final positions carry z = 0.75 and no other value); the apple is Sphere((0.25,0.15,0.79), r=0.04) (flyverse/world.py:338) and flyverse/batch_sim.py:151-155 returns |p-centre| - radius, so the ground floor is exactly sqrt(0.04^2) - 0.04 = 0.0000 cm. Empirically all 20 flies with a negative minimum took off (20/20), and all 65 flies that never took off bottom out at +0.597 cm. The premise ('no sphere collision for a walking fly') is true in x,y but cannot produce a negative surface distance; the whole negative tail is airborne.
* 'Nothing supports slowing the drain IN ORDER TO make feeding measurable' and P3's 'lowering the drain moves food-finding the WRONG way' (on meals). The audit's drain conclusion compares drain 0.5/0.25 at brain seed 0 against a 26-batch pool of other seeds. The seed-matched comparison was available in its own files (the 300 s checkpoint of its 10-min runs) and was not used for this: at brain seed 1 / env 16-31, 300 s, halving the drain takes meals per fly 0.312 -> 0.500 (default) and 0.250 -> 0.500 (off), flies fed 5 -> 7 and 4 -> 8 of 16, and the closest-approach median 6.20 -> 2.52 cm and 7.12 -> 2.31 cm. At brain seed 0 / env 0-15 it does not (default 0.250 -> 0.188, off 0.000 -> 0.250). Three of the four seed-matched drain-1.0 -> drain-0.5 transitions on file raise meals. The drain's direction on food-finding is unresolved, not established as adverse.
* 'meals is 0.125-0.375 per fly everywhere' (SUMMARY) and 'Highest single run 0.375' (KEY CLAIM 1). Two of my four new runs give 0.500 meals per fly with 8 of 16 flies fed, at drain-scale 0.5 / brain seed 1: out/skfeed_default_d0.5_5min_s1.json and out/skfeed_off_d0.5_5min_s1.json. The central negative ('never countable', i.e. < 1 per fly) survives at 0.500, but the quoted range and maximum do not.
* 'close enough that the assay, not the searcher, is the binding constraint' (section 1.2), and calling the random-sweep figure 'the assay's geometric ceiling' / 'No horizon below ~17 minutes can give one encounter per fly'. The protocol starts every fly at (-0.15, 0.15), exactly on the apple's y axis (0.25, 0.15), 40 cm straight downwind of it. A straight upwind walker holding heading within +/-5.39 deg reaches the 3.775 cm capture disc in 29.6 s = 0.49 min, not 17.3 min; the capture disc subtends 10.78 deg (3.00 % of headings) from the start. The 17.3-minute figure is a blind-random-sweep baseline, not a ceiling for an odour-guided fly whose own rule is 'steer upwind'. Running at 43-53 % of a blind sweep indicts the searcher -- which is what section 6 concludes, so 1.2 contradicts the audit's own answer.
* 'a grep finds exactly four consumers of `energy` (hunger at body.py:186, the satiety/resume hysteresis at :191-201, the HUD string at :206, room_ui.py:445)'. flyverse/batch_body.py:110-120 is a fifth consumer -- and it is the one that actually executed in all twelve batch jobs (the scalar Metabolism.update at body.py:188-202 never runs under BatchSim). The substantive conclusion is unaffected: I read batch_body._metabolism and it also has no death, no immobilisation and no episode termination at energy 0.
* 'Measured residuals ... always negative' (5.1 item 6 and KEY CLAIM 14). One of the 8 qualifying flies in out/feedh_default_d0.25_5min_s0.json has a residual of +4.54e-06 -- the audit's own quoted range ('-2.7e-03 to +4.5e-06') contradicts its 'always negative'.

### Corrections
* The stratified statistic and the Fisher combination -- the audit's headline p 0.016 -- are in no shipped script (probe_feeding_horizon.py only calls scipy.stats.mannwhitneyu at :477), which breaks 'ship every generator into scripts/'. My independent version agrees in sign and magnitude (z -2.506 p 0.0122 vs their -2.42 p 0.016). Separately, the p is a 32-flies-per-arm statistic: at the replicate level the nonparametric floor is p 0.125 with four pairs (their data) and p 0.2188 with six (theirs plus mine), and the 10-min cell alone permutes to exactly 2/6 = 0.333. The audit quotes asymptotic p for its main table and exact p for its never-took-off control (0.212 / 0.238 match scipy's exact method; the asymptotic values are 0.203 / 0.232) -- both defensible, but the mixture should be stated. The exact meals p-values are more conservative than the quoted ones (0.544 / 0.780 / 0.780 vs 0.402 / 0.693 / 0.653), which only strengthens the negative.
* 'path_m separates the models cleanly ... the default walks 2-4 % further, which is a locomotor difference' is not established. path accumulates ||delta(x,y)|| over every frame including airborne ones (probe_feeding_horizon.py:188-189), and the arms differ 15x in take-offs. At 10 minutes the entire arm-level gap (0.1582 m per fly, 5.063 m over 32 flies) sits inside the ballistic budget of the 72 extra hops: it needs only 0.378 m/s averaged over the 0.419 s per fly of extra airborne time, against Flight.jump_speed 0.6 m/s at 45 deg pitch with drag 2.5/s (~6 cm per hop, ~4.6 m over the arm). Neither of my new cells reproduces the significance (p 0.376 and p 0.158). The drain-0.5 gap is too large for that accounting (~35 %), so this is a quantified caveat rather than a clean refutation. The before/after speed claim is NOT affected -- the off arm shows the same 12-15 % rise with 5 take-offs in 32 flies.
* THE APPROACH MEASURE IS ENTANGLED WITH THE TAKE-OFF MEASURE, more deeply than 'it includes airborne frames' conveys. Within each arm, hopping predicts a closer approach: Spearman(hops, min_distance) rho -0.517 p 0.0024 (off, 10 min), -0.751 p 0.0008 (off, d0.5), -0.346 p 0.0521 (default, 10 min), pooled cell-z-scored -0.248 p 0.049 (default) and -0.384 p 0.0017 (off). Across all 128 rollouts, 27 of 63 hoppers came inside 1.5 cm and 23 fed, against 4 of 65 never-hoppers. Causality is unresolvable from these files (approaching a 4 cm sphere should loom and trigger a GF escape, which would make proximity cause hops rather than the reverse), but recommendation (A) would install as the primary score a quantity co-determined by the one arm difference that is already the most robust on file (hops, p 2.7e-11). Clipping the impossible negative tail at the ground floor leaves the direction intact (gap 2.060 vs 2.402 cm at 10 min, p 0.0472 vs 0.0419; stratified z -2.452 vs -2.506), so the airborne tail alone is not what breaks the result -- replication is.
* The section-2.1 shell enrichment is quoted against a uniform point and then carried into the ANSWER as 'enriched 3-4x in the 12-25 cm shells'. Against an x-matched null (observed final-x marginal, y uniform), which isolates the plume-specific lateral signature, the 12-16 cm enrichment falls from 4.32x to 2.21x, 16-20 from 3.45x to 1.65x, 20-25 from 3.16x to 1.53x, 'within 20 cm' from 2.81x to 1.62x -- and inside 12 cm there is no enrichment at all (1.07x) and inside 8 cm a depletion (0.86x). The audit states the caveat in words ('a fly walking blindly upwind would also end at large x') but never quantifies it. This strengthens its terminal-approach conclusion while removing the 'flies reach the source region' framing.
* Scope and label slips, each verified: (a) 'meals is an arrival counter thresholded at 1.5 cm' needs 'and on the ground' -- batch_sim.py:174 ANDs the 1.5 cm test with ~airborne. (b) 'median over 3-13 qualifying flies per arm' for the drain identity: four of the eight arms have zero qualifying flies (every 10-minute fly fed, hopped or hit the floor), so the check covers only the 5-minute arms. (c) Section 2.1 calls 10.9 % 'in the search program' while 5.1 establishes that the armed fraction is 'exploring' (50.9 %) -- the two are contradictory. (d) Option (B) says drain-scale 0.5 would leave benchmark.py's window at 'gain 0.56'; the same arithmetic gives 0.1 + 0.9*(1-0.5124) = 0.539, and P3 gets 0.54 right for the identical number. (e) Section 6 pairs the 10-minute approach numbers with '29 % of every rollout ... at surge-level odour', but the 10-minute runs are 21.1-23.0 % surging; 29.14 % is the 5-minute 416-rollout figure. (f) The drain-1.0 path reference '3.67-3.75 m per 300 s' comes from other scripts' batches; the probe's own 300 s checkpoints give 3.557-3.699 m, so the quoted path cost of 6 % and 13 % is 5.3 % and 11.0 % on its own instrument. (g) Citations off by one: the np.where(x > 0, ...) is air.py:104 not :103, the sig formula is air.py:102 (not the :52-55 docstring it is attributed to), and attr() is batch_body.py:12-13 not :13-14.
* Two instrumentation gaps relevant to the skeptic checklist. The run JSON records options.device = None -- the REQUEST, not the realised device; device=cuda appears only in the .txt and console logs (all 8 + 4 jobs verified there). And the JSON carries no LIFParams dump, so 'print LIFParams from the run's JSON' cannot be done; I established from the code instead that patch_receptor (:71-83) sets only receptor_model and receptor_net_rule and that no gain is touched anywhere in the script, and cross-checked receptor.fast_sign_changed_entries = 48,295 of 25,578,600 for the default arms and 0 for off, plus flight.gf_hz 33.0 everywhere. A future version should dump LIFParams and sim.fb.device.
* Process note, including my own slip. The audit says the --drain-scale 0 hunger clamp was 'verified ... 2 flies x 0.6 s on CPU'; the probe auto-selects a device, and my identical rerun on this desktop printed device=cuda -- so either they passed --device cpu (not recorded in the audit) or that smoke ran on the desktop GPU. I ran the same 2-fly 0.6 s command before noticing, which is a CLUSTER RULE slip on my part (1.2 fly-s); I did no further local simulation and put the replicate batch through scripts/cluster_run.py. Also worth recording: the handover asked for three brain seeds at the 10-minute horizon and the audit ran two, spending the other four jobs on the drain arms.
* Files I produced: scripts/sk_feeding_horizon_verify.py (CPU-only, sections A-F, reproduces every number above from the audit's own JSONs and adds the four controls; run with --glob 'feedh_*.json,skfeed_*.json'), out/sk_feed_cluster.log (batch sk-feed-048583, 4 jobs, 0 failed, 55.0 min, all device=cuda), and out/skfeed_default_d1.0_5min_s2.{json,txt}, out/skfeed_off_d1.0_5min_s2.{json,txt}, out/skfeed_default_d0.5_5min_s1.{json,txt}, out/skfeed_off_d0.5_5min_s1.{json,txt}. I edited none of the feeding thread's files and nothing in flyverse/.

## verify:optic-audit  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* "walk.power_max fails under 14 of 16 ablations (51.5-77.8 Hz)" (KEY CLAIM 6 / ANSWER) is 13 of 16. Recomputed from every out/optic_audit/<cfg>/<cfg>.json `checks` array: FAIL at 51.51, 51.70, 55.26, 59.63, 60.44, 60.75, 63.33, 63.82, 66.97, 70.66, 70.90, 77.85, 198.77; PASS at pair_gain_lpi_x2 48.12, norm_l1 46.65, no_drive_clip 49.25. The band "51.5-77.8" also drops the 13th failure (out_norm_l2 198.77). docs/audits/optic_measures.md:139 itself says "every ablation but three" (= 13), so the doc is right and the reported claim miscounts.
* "LPi x1 / x2 ... the round-4 damped-gain values bit for bit" (KEY CLAIM 5 / ANSWER; docs/audits/optic_measures.md:135-137 "the same numbers") holds for walk.power_max only. Exact values in out/optic_audit/pair_gain_lpi_x{1,2}/*.json: power_max 51.50775146484375 / 48.12049865722656 vs round 4's 51.5078 / 48.1205 (anti_runaway.md:412) -- bit-identical. But walk.GF_max is 9.795085906982422 here vs 9.7681 in round 4 (anti_runaway.md:413), and walk_gf.p99 is 26.7633 here against round 4's 26.98-31.14 range (below it) while x2's 32.1787 is above round 4's 23.63-31.09. Two of the three quoted x1 numbers are not the round-4 numbers and the p99 ranges do not even overlap.
* "the none-vs-none null |z| <= 0.9 everywhere" / "|z| <= 0.9 for every type in every seed of both stimuli" (KEY CLAIM 7; docs/audits/optic_measures.md:165) is contradicted by the cited files. From out/optic_audit/baseline/stages_s{0,1,2}.json the per-seed |z(C-B)| reaches 5.53 (MeVP11 signed), 5.25 (TmY17), 4.56 (LC25), 3.42 (Tm31), 3.22 (MeLo3b) in the ball map and 1.83 (Pm10 abs) in the apple map; the largest seed-mean |z(C-B)| is 1.95 (MeLo3b, ball) and 1.09 (LLPC3, apple). My independent rerun of seed 0 gives MeVP11 ball C-B = +4.20 where the recorded run gives -5.53. What is true: no type reaches |z(C-B)| >= 3 in every seed, and the ~35 named types of the apple map have mean |z(C-B)| <= 0.9.
* "with gain_fb = 0 every none-vs-none statistic is exactly 0.0000" / "the none-vs-none pair is bit-identical in every statistic of every type" (KEY CLAIM 9; optic_measures.md:221-222) is false at face value. In out/optic_audit/no_spk_feedback/obj_null_s0.json 16 of 78 diff statistics are non-zero: LC11 diff_max_over_cells 5.18e-09 mV, diff_peak_100ms 2.62e-07, diff_tuning_peak 5.14e-08, diff_mean_over_cells 1.28e-11 (LC10b, LPLC2, LC4 likewise), and the values differ between s0 and s1 (5.18e-09 vs 5.14e-09) and between the recorded run and my rerun (max delta 4.8e-09). The null is ~1e-9 to 3e-7 mV -- five to seven orders below the shipped null (0.06 mV), i.e. numerically zero but not bit-identical.
* "the two ball seeds are identical" (KEY CLAIM 9) holds only for the headline statistic. Diffing out/optic_audit/no_spk_feedback/obj_ball_s0.json against obj_ball_s1.json: diff_max_over_cells_mean_mv is identical for all 6 LC types, but LC10b rate_hz_mean 1.65965 vs 1.68158, rate_hz_max_cell 7.667 vs 7.750, cells_over_1hz 49 vs 48; LPLC2 cells_over_1hz 7 vs 6; drive_mean_mv differs at ~1e-11. The rate optic lobe is deterministic; the spiking network is not, even at gain_fb = 0.
* "none of the 16 ablations makes these types carry" / "no ablation makes them carry" (KEY CLAIM 8 / ANSWER) is contradicted by the report's own carry flags: in out/optic_audit/report.md norm_l1 / apple, T2 is True/True (+5.4 signed, +6.3 abs), T3 True/True (-4.6, +7.9) and TmY13 False/True (+5.1). The disclosed reason (a quiet linear lobe) is right, and the magnitude collapse is larger than the doc's "10x": from stages_s*.json the apple signed figure at T2 is 4.8e-05 (norm_l1) vs 5.5e-03 (baseline) -- ~110x -- with sd_bg down from 2.2e-03 to 9.1e-06 (~240x); T3 2.7e-05 vs 1.1e-03.
* "Not load-bearing for any scored check: LPi x4" (KEY CLAIM 5 / ANSWER) is false as stated: pair_gain_lpi_x1 is 10 pass / 1 FAIL, the FAIL being walk.power_max 51.5078 against the < 50 bound -- the identical number round 4 used to conclude the opposite ("cannot be retired: breaks walk.power_max (51.56 in 3/3)", anti_runaway.md:302). Against a baseline recorded at 48.4805 in 14/14 draws with zero scatter, the +3.0 Hz rise is a real effect that crosses a scored bound; what the audit disputes is the bound's meaning, not the measurement. The same wording is used for spiking feedback, adaptation and gain_in, whose only FAIL is that check.
* "the walk / motion sections draw no RNG, so their differences between configurations are bit-stable" (optic_measures.md:347-349 -- the premise that licenses one benchmark draw per configuration) is refuted by an independent cluster rerun of the identical configuration (my out/optic_verify_cluster.log, run optic-verify-acf696, 3 jobs, 0 failed, NVIDIA B200, same cache sum|W| 121,460,584, same seeds 0,1). pair_gain_lpi_x1 recorded vs rerun: walk.GF_max 9.79509 / 9.79509, power_max 51.5078 / 51.5078, power_sustained 20.2129 / 20.2129 (bit-identical) BUT loom.GF_peak 59.5391 / 52.7937 (-6.75 Hz), rotate.DNp20_flip -34.7047 / -24.2460 (+10.46 Hz), loom_escape.GF_peak 48.1493 / 54.0282 (+5.88 Hz; per-seed 43.76/48.15 -> 53.0/54.0), motion.min_dsi 0.246778 / 0.237748 (-0.009), walk_gf.p99 26.7633 / 26.3640. So the motion section is NOT bit-stable and the loom / rotate sub-sections swing by 6-10 Hz within one configuration. Consequences: the quoted loom.GF_peak differences (e.g. "LPi x1/x2 raise the legacy peak 59.5 / 51.4 vs 47.2") and the rotate.DNp20_flip table ("the rest -26 to -45, baseline -34") are inside a single configuration's own run-to-run swing, and no_t4_pair_gain's "min_dsi 0.099 FAIL" on a 0.10 bound is not a reproducible FAIL (one rerun moved min_dsi by 0.009 and T4a's subtype DSI from 0.3907 to ~0.41).
* The figure-stage map is not reproducible at fixed seed, which the report does not state. Rerunning probe_figure_stages for baseline seed 0 on the cluster (out/optic_verify/baseline/stages_s0.json vs out/optic_audit/baseline/stages_s0.json, same stage_source "type-name family rule", same B200): 234 apple and 376 ball per-type z(A-B) values compared, median |dz| 0.36 / 0.49, p90 1.34 / 1.70, max 5.24 (MeLo7 signed +0.52 -> +5.76) / 7.56; the |z| >= 3 single-seed threshold flips for 15 of 234 apple type-measures (Dm18, Dm3a, Dm3b, L1 abs 3.98 -> 2.79, L4, MeLo7, Mi4 abs 3.72 -> 3.00, Mi9, Pm4, Pm5, Tm2 ...). The figures themselves move (Tm3 apple -1.508e-02 -> -1.740e-02; MeLo7 1.28e-03 -> 6.34e-03), not only sd_bg. The baseline object sweep behaves the same way at fixed seed 0: LC10b ball 0.0835 -> 0.2514 (3x), LC11 ball 0.0788 -> 0.1201, LPLC2 null 0.2693 -> 0.2286, while the rate types are stable (Mi4 0.0482 -> 0.0464, Tm3 0.1082 -> 0.1010). So the "3 seeds" are 3 draws of a pipeline whose fixed-seed reproducibility is as poor as its seed-to-seed spread, and every z quoted to 0.1 and every borderline carry call is unstable.

### Corrections
* Because the pipeline is not reproducible at fixed seed (see the refutations), the honest replicate unit is 'runs', not 'seeds', and the right statements are the coarse ones: the carriers stay far above the criterion across draws (L1 15.9-20.9, L2 10.5-13.4, Mi1 -8.2 to -10.1, Tm3 -5.4 to -8.6, Tm20 4.6-6.2, LPLC2 3.45-3.63) and the LC10/LC11 inputs stay far below it (Tm5Y 0.1-0.5, TmY21 -0.6, T2 1.7-2.2, T3 -0.6 to -2.5, LC10a +-0.2). Every per-type z quoted to 0.1, every per-stage carry COUNT, and the marginal calls (T2 +2.7, T4b +2.6/+2.9, T4c min 3.59 -> 3.16 on rerun) should be read as +-1.5, and no_t4_pair_gain's min_dsi 0.099 should be reported as 'on the bound, not a reproducible FAIL'.
* The pooling arithmetic that carries the LC10/LC11 conclusion -- 'an LC11 cell pools 94 columns (median; LC10a 32, LC10b 41, LPLC2 86) with 30 % of its optic input from its best three columns (LC10a 48 %, LPLC2 34 %)' (optic_measures.md:261-262) -- is in no shipped file, log or generator; it violates the round's 'every number from a named file or run' rule. My CPU proxy on the shipped cache (median presynaptic rate cells per cell, top-3-cell |W| share) gives LC11 454 / 4.3 %, LC10a 128 / 10.7 %, LC10b 151 / 10.1 %, LPLC2 428 / 4.1 %, LC16 122 / 11.0 %, LC4 372 / 4.9 % -- a consistent ~4-5 cells per claimed column, so the numbers are plausible but unverified; ship the generator.
* All 17 shipped runs used the family rule for staging, not the Nern table ('stage_source': 'type-name family rule' in every stages_s*.json, because data/external is not shipped to the cluster), while the doc says the stage assignment IS the Nern figure groups. The difference is not cosmetic: 14 ol_intrinsic types the table stages as VPN (H1, LC14a-1/2, LC14b, LT54, MeVPLp2, MeVPMe5/7/10/12/13, MeVPOL1, dCal1, l-LNv) get no stage under the family rule and were therefore unscored in every map.
* 'a x5 on 46 % of T5's input of both signs': the 46 % is the cholinergic share alone (Tm9 28.5 + Tm4 17.3 of T5a). The both-signs share is 72.7 % for T5a and 38.9 % across T5a-d (check.json share_of_post_l2_input_rr = 0.3887). Also the T5a percentages are cited to check.json but live in connectivity_structure.json.
* The pass/fail/gap tally under-counts: the generator counts only status == 'KNOWN GAP' (scripts/audit_optic.py:308) while the missing loom.escape_cm has status 'MISSING', so no_pair_gain prints 5/5/0 and norm_l1 6/4/0 -- 10 of their 11 checks. Both should read 5/5/1 and 6/4/1.
* 'the static-apple figure collapses (L1 +3.6 vs +18.4 ...)' for no_optic_adapt mixes measures: +3.6 is the abs z and +18.4 the signed z. The signed pair is +1.4 vs +18.4 (the abs pair is +3.6 vs +4.2) -- the collapse is bigger than stated, but the comparison as printed is not like-for-like.
* Two hand-set OpticParams are outside the 15-entry inventory: gain_rr = 1.0 (the recurrent optic-lobe gain, never mentioned in the doc) and the global baseline = 0.5 (only inside row #7). gain_rr is a no-op at 1.0 like entry #5, but it multiplies exactly the term that entry #11's adaptation exists to tame ('recurrent gain > 1'), so it belongs in the list. Separately, optic.py's own comment on entry #4 reads 'T4/T5 outputs x4' while the shipped factor is 2.0 -- the same class of mis-description the audit caught for #2, and it is not flagged.
* An A/B asymmetry in the static-apple protocol is not disclosed: run_apple builds the stimulus arm with fruit_set='apple' (an apple 9 cm away, so its ODOUR is present) and the none arms with fruit_set='all' plus every fruit teleported to (9,9,9) (scripts/probe_figure_stages.py:209-217). (A-B) therefore contains an olfactory difference as well as a visual one, and the (C-B) null does not control for it. The retinotopic subtraction removes its spatially uniform part, which is most of it, but this should be stated as a limit (or the arms matched) since the odour reaches the lobe through the same gain_fb path the report identifies as the noise source.
* 'TmY21 is fed by non-carriers (TmY5a, TmY13, Tm5a, Tm5Y)' omits its third input, Tm20 at 5.8 %, which is a carrier (z +5.3) and supplies its largest positive contribution (+0.000615); and 'no hand-crafted measure sits on these edges' has the two exceptions the doc itself lists elsewhere (T5b -> T2 2.1 % and T5d -> TmY5a 4.5 %, both at the x2 output gain). The loss mechanism is also not one mechanism: T3/T2 are cancellation (contributions ~0.001 summing to -0.0004), whereas Tm5Y is dilution plus higher background scatter (14 % of a +0.0107 carrier, estimate +0.00084, sd_bg 3.9e-3).
* On the LPLC2 null rise, the exact tie-aware test the round asks for: rank-sum of the 3 shipped-gain null draws (0.154, 0.269, 0.291) against round 3's 5 damped-gain draws (0.081, 0.124, 0.137, 0.174, 0.178) gives U = 13 of 15, one-sided p = 4/56 = 0.071; the ball arm's apparent drop is p = 0.29. So 'the null rose while the ball arm did not move' is the right description but is not significant at n = 3 vs 5 -- stronger than the doc's 'not settled', and worth stating as p = 0.07.
* Two further reading caveats on the benchmark half: loom_escape.GF_peak is a max over seeds taken over 3 draws for the baseline and 2 for every ablation (a max over more draws is biased upward, and my rerun showed a 5.9 Hz swing within one configuration), and loom.escape_cm passes on 'notnone', so the runaway configuration out_norm_l2 scores a PASS at 50.0 cm and no_t4t5_rectify at 9.0 cm. Neither is flagged.
* My verification artefacts, for the record (no file of the reported thread was edited): out/optic_verify_cluster.log (cluster run optic-verify-acf696, 3 jobs, 0 failed, 14.5 min, all NVIDIA B200 / torch 2.11.0+cu128), out/optic_verify/{baseline/{stages_s0.json,obj_ball_s0.json,obj_null_s0.json}, pair_gain_lpi_x1/pair_gain_lpi_x1.json, no_spk_feedback/obj_{ball,null}_s{0,1}.json} plus the three job console logs; the comparison and re-derivation scripts are in C:/Users/ethee/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/D--Projects-flyverse/d280c0e1-e89c-49ce-943c-279a614bcc17/scratchpad (chk1-13.py, cmp.py, cmp2.py, cmp3.py, pool.py, stage2.py, mw.py). The reruns used the reported thread's own generator, scripts/audit_optic.py --one.

## verify:takeoff-hold  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* THE ANSWER'S QUANTIFIER ("the OPTIC side, effectively all of it"; "the optic side carries the ENTIRE room take-off cost") and claim 4's shares as stable estimates. I ran an independent 4th replicate of ALL FOUR arms in ONE cluster submission at a brain seed / env block used by no audited batch (brain seed 3, env 48-63; 5 jobs, 0 failed, 56.2 min, run dir $CLUSTER_RUNS/sk-d1-hold-f691cf, exit 0 in 5/5, torch 2.11.0+cu128 NVIDIA B200; D:/Projects/flyverse/scripts/sk_d1_hold_verify.sh, out/sk_d1_hold_cluster.log, out/sk_d1_{shipped,holdBrain,holdOptic,off}_4.json). My batch: shipped 19 = 3 esc + 16 vol, GF med 31.56, 3/16; holdBrain (optic side) 7 = 1 + 6, 30.29, 1/16; holdOptic (Brain side) 3 = 3 + 0, 25.95, 3/16; off 4 = 4 + 0, 27.76, 4/16. There the optic side is 0.37x the default on hops (7 vs 19, binomial p 0.029) and reproduces 20 % of the hop excess and 37 % of the voluntary excess, not ~100 %. Pooled over 4 matched batches per arm (19,200 fly-s each; out/sk_d1_hold_pool.log): shipped 75 = 27 + 48, holdBrain 59 = 19 + 40, holdOptic 8 = 8 + 0, off 11 = 11 + 0, and the shares move from 92 / 65 / 106 / 103 % to 75 % (hops), 50 % (escape), 83 % (voluntary), 95 % (GF-median shift). The three-batch shares are single-batch-noise-dominated point estimates that moved 17-20 points on one added replicate; 'most of it, indistinguishable from all of it at this exposure' is supported, 'the entire cost' is not.
* THE ESCAPE-ROUTE HALF of the attribution (claim 1's escape 1.250 vs 1.667, claim 4's 'escape 65 %', and 'the entire walking-GF tail' read as escape counts). With my 4th matched batch added, the optic side's escape excess over off is no longer significant: holdBrain vs off escape U 2300.0 p 0.0948 (three batches: U 1410.5 p 0.0108), 19 vs 11 counts, ratio to the default 0.704x [0.388, 1.257]. The escape excess being attributed has itself shrunk to 2.45x (shipped 27 vs off 11 over 19,200 fly-s each; binomial p 0.0139, CI 1.25-5.08x) from the 3.43x on record, and in my env block the default's escape count (3) was BELOW off's (4). At this exposure the escape route cannot be attributed to either side; only the voluntary route (48 vs 0; holdBrain 40 vs off 0, U 3040 p 2.7e-10) and the GF median carry the attribution.

### Corrections
* Quote the precision, not 'all of it'. The design's resolution at 14,400 fly-s per arm is coarse: an arm is indistinguishable from the default for counts 37-79 of 56 on hops (0.66-1.41x), 12-40 of 24 on escape (0.50-1.67x) and 18-50 of 32 on voluntary (0.56-1.56x). So the three-batch result bounds the optic side's share only to roughly two-thirds-or-more, while it bounds the Brain side's tightly (<= 0.21x hops, <= 0.08x voluntary). The honest headline is asymmetric: the BRAIN SIDE CARRIES NONE OF IT is a strong, replicated result; THE OPTIC SIDE CARRIES MOST OF IT is the matching weak one.
* The 'four measures' are two channels, so 'indistinguishable on all four' / 'separated on all four' overstates the independent evidence. hops = escape + voluntary by construction, and 'rows >= 33 Hz' is IDENTICAL to 'rows with at least one escape hop' -- 47 = 47 with 0 disagreements over all 192 rows I checked. The GF-median shift (103 %) and the escape count share (65 %) are two summaries of the same channel; that they disagree is itself the evidence that the 65 % is count noise, which supports the report's dismissal but should be said that way.
* The escape/voluntary split is not two independent counters: flyverse/batch_body.py:84-87 gives escape PRIORITY (voluntary = ~airborne & ~escape & hold >= 0.3 s). An arm with a higher GF therefore pre-empts voluntary launches, so holdBrain's lower escape (18 vs 24) with higher voluntary (34 vs 32) at a nearly equal total (52 vs 56) is the expected trade-off, not two independent deviations. This mechanism explains the report's one anomalous number and should be stated instead of attributing it to scatter alone.
* Dose is not controlled. The optic half is 92 % of the changed entries and 95 % of the changed |W| (44,463/48,295; 171,111/179,944). Nothing in this section separates 'the optic PATHWAY carries it' from 'the larger perturbation carries it'. Round 5's finding that the 3,832-entry Brain side carries 100 % of taste and smell shows the small side is not globally inert, which mitigates but does not close it; the report's own proposed next step (holdOpticHis / holdOpticGlu) is the right control and should be flagged as required, not optional.
* 'Brain side' means 'postsynaptic superclass outside ol_intrinsic / ol_sensory', and 94 of its 3,832 entries land on visual centrifugal / visual projection cells (OA-AL2i3 65, TmY14 29). 'The Brain side carries nothing' therefore does not mean 'no visual neuron outside the medulla matters'; it means that partition as a whole carries nothing.
* '0 voluntary in 43,200 fly-s over 9 batches' undercounts the record. out/sk5_sustain_off_live_1r.json and out/r5_sustain_off_nogf_{1,2,3}.json are also off batches with 0 voluntary, and my out/sk_d1_off_4.json adds a further 4,800 fly-s: off now has 0 voluntary take-offs in 11 batches / 52,800 fly-s.
* Three of the section's own artefacts ship no generator: out/d1_hold_structure.log, out/d1_hold_weights.log and out/d1_walk_gfmax_recount.log (the doc explains that the task's file list allowed only scripts/batch_sustain.py under scripts/, and it did ship out/d1_hold_summary.py and the two .sh). Nothing in them is wrong -- I reproduced all three independently, including the shaped-weight |W| -- but they are not re-runnable from the repo as the round's rule asks. My re-derivations are shipped as scripts/sk_d1_hold_{structure,weights,stats,pool}.py -> out/sk_d1_hold_{structure,weights,stats,pool}.log.
* |W| bookkeeping is two different measures presented as one. out/d1_hold_weights.log's 175,706 / 167,029 / 8,678 are SHAPED-weight |W| (I reproduced them exactly: 167,029 + 8,678 = 175,707); the claims quote raw synapse |W| 179,944 / 171,111 / 8,833. Both are correct; the report never says the weights log measures a different quantity than the table above it.
* No shipped artefact records the run-dir md5 listing or the '9/9 md5-verified after the fetch' check -- those live only in the doc's prose (the task asked for LIFParams printed from the run's JSON, and the room JSONs carry no LIFParams or type_path_gain block, as the report itself notes via anti_runaway.md caveat (1)). scripts/batch_sustain.py is also missing from the 'byte-identical on all four code files' list, and the reference arm demonstrably ran an OLDER copy (its options block has no receptor_table key). Behaviour-preserving, as I verified, but it should be said, and batch_sustain's JSON header should carry type_path_gain / the resolved LIFParams so the next round does not depend on prose.
* Two small overstatements. (a) '92 min' is 91.2 min by the run dir's own logs/*.times (min start 1789238065.71, max end 1789243535.67); 7 jobs ran 5,450-5,470 s and 2 ran ~2,915 s. (b) 'all device=cuda B200': this batch's joblogs and JSONs record only 'device=cuda' -- no GPU model string appears anywhere in the d1 artefacts. (I confirmed B200 independently from my own run, so the fact is right, the citation is not.)
* 'Kruskal-Wallis n.s. for every count metric' is true at alpha 0.05 but marginal where it matters least-well: holdOptic hops and escape give H 5.684 p 0.0583, and on the GF tail off-shipped p 0.0635 and shipped p 0.1003. 'The three brain RNGs behave as replicates of one rate' rests on that borderline test in the small-count arms.
* The report's central comparison is the only one drawn ACROSS run directories and submissions -- the shipped-default arm is r5-adopt-fb3608 from nine hours earlier, and the d1 batch contains only holdBrain / holdOptic / off. Code identity is established by md5, but GPU allocation, contention and driver state are not. My replication puts all four arms in one submission at matched env seeds, which is the control this claim needs; its result is the refutation above. Also note the report omits the 4th shipped-default batch already on record (out/r5_skeptic_sustain_live_4.json, 11 hops at the same seed/env as batch 1): including it would have moved the shares the other way, to 104 / 83 / 113 %, so the choice was conservative -- but the two-sided sensitivity (104 % with that batch, 75 % with mine) is the real message: these shares are not measured to better than a factor ~1.5 by three batches.

# Object round 2 (2026-09-13) -- build skeptic verdicts

Object round 2 built three tools for Neurome's size-tuning intake (`docs/NEUROME_INTERFACE.md` 3b): the
matched ray-traced sphere assay (`build:sphere`), the synthetic radiance stimuli + per-body RF localizer
(`build:synthetic`) and the opt-in per-stream optic hooks (`build:hooks`), each checked by an Opus skeptic.
All three verdicts **mostly sound**. Verdicts verbatim, as in rounds 3-5 and dynamics round 1. The
corrections were applied to the threads' own documents (`object_matched_assay.md`,
`object_synthetic_stimuli.md`, `optic_stream_hooks.md`, `docs/INTERP.md` 2.7) by this round's critic; this
section is the record, not the application.

## verify:sphere  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

(the matched ray-traced sphere assay, `scripts/probe_object_matched.py`)

### Refuted
*(the verdict's list is empty -- nothing under this heading)*

### Corrections
* MAIN GAP -- effective retinal CONTRAST is not matched across the ladder, and the audit does not say so. Measured by me from the captured radiance (out/objm/smk2/*.npz): the median per-frame extreme relative luminance change is -0.475 / -0.889 / -0.935 / -0.954 at 4.5 / 11 / 20 / 30 deg, and the mean over the dimmed set -0.59 / -0.76 / -0.82 / -0.86. The ball's MATERIAL is identical across rungs; the realised Weber contrast in the best column grows monotonically with angular size because a 4.5-deg ball only partially fills a column at 4.6-deg spacing. This is precisely the confound Neurome flagged in the intake ('increase in retinal contrast footprint may contribute to the drive ordering', line 119) and listed among the things to control ('trajectory, speed, contrast and background controlled', line 193). No KEY CLAIM asserts matched contrast, so nothing is false -- but the report's framing ('the MATCHED visual assay that answers Neurome's experiment 1') overstates coverage on that one item, and docs/audits/object_matched_assay.md section 5 names the related 4.5-deg undersampling without naming this. The ladder that follows will still confound size with effective contrast at the small end unless a per-rung contrast readout is added to the predeclared reading rules (the data are already in the npz).
* The bright-object control is not contrast-matched to the dark one. At the same 11 deg, `lamp` gives a median extreme relative change of +0.511 and strongly changes 1.217 columns/frame; `black` gives -0.889 and 3.380 columns/frame (|rel|>0.25: 2.353 vs 4.390). Claim 6 only says the tracer 'supports the bright-object option', which is true and verified -- but a bright/dark specificity comparison run at the shipped settings would compare two different effective contrasts, which should be stated before it is used as one of Neurome's specificity controls.
* Claim 10's wall time is load-dependent, not a property of the box. The build's own smk2 runs are 9.23-9.28 s/arm (H200) and 10.41-10.48 s/arm (B200); in my 10-job batch on the same boxes the same 3 s configuration took 15.6-18.5 s/arm on B200 and 5.7-9.7 s/arm on H200. Quote a range (roughly 6-19 s/arm for 600 frames, depending on concurrency), not '~9-10 s'. The follow-on '~3-4 min for a 12 s ladder job' is an untested extrapolation (the 3 s smoke jobs took 66-86 s wall each end-to-end).
* Claim 1's old-ladder numbers have no shipped generator. `probe_object_matched.py geometry` computes only the MATCHED geometry -- it has no ball-on-table model and no lateral-line sweep, so it cannot produce +0.92 / +4.35 / +8.51 / +13.71 deg, 11.4 -> 7.3 deg, or 45.8 -> 18.8 deg/s. I reproduced all of them by hand from probe_object_sweep.py's constants and they are arithmetically right, but the round's rule is 'ship every generator of every quoted number into scripts/'. Add an `oldladder` subcommand (or a flag on `geometry`) that prints that table from probe_object_sweep's BALL_R / AHEAD / HALF_SWEEP / SWEEP_S / eye_height.
* Claim 4's 'dimmed band +-1.5 / +-5.3 / +-9 / +-15 deg symmetric about 0' rounds away a real asymmetry at the large rungs: the measured bands are [-1.49, +1.65], [-5.25, +5.41], [-9.85, +8.55], [-15.29, +14.61] deg, i.e. band centres 0.00 / +0.08 / -0.65 / -0.34 deg. Still far inside one interommatidial angle (4.6 deg), but '+-9' hides [-9.85, +8.55]. Also, the '0.83 deg' spread printed by verify is over all six non-null runs (it includes the lamp and ceiling variants); the four dark ladder rungs alone spread 0.787 deg.
* Provenance caveat the report does not name: the smoke was produced from a working tree carrying two OTHER tasks' uncommitted changes to flyverse/optic.py (the hooks refactor, +215/-13 lines touching the shipped optic path) and flyverse/fly.py (proprioception). The cluster log confirms those files were shipped to the boxes. `provenance.flyverse_commit` on the box reads {commit: 'unknown', dirty: false, modified_files: []}, which understates this -- only `source_fingerprint` (per-file sha256, computed true) makes it recoverable. So the sphere numbers rest on the hooks task's separate bit-identity claim, which I did not verify here. This is common.provenance's behaviour on a git-less box, not a bug in the sphere probe, but the audit should say which tree the smoke ran on. (For the record: the four hook fields are null in every run JSON and `git diff -- flyverse/brain.py flyverse/body.py` is empty; `git diff -- flyverse/fly.py` is NOT empty, but its content is the proprioception task's, not this build's.)
* Extra data from my replicate batch, offered for the ladder task and NOT claimed by the build (exploratory, not a predeclared Holm family, one diameter, 3 s windows, out/objm/skept, 5 v 5 in one submission, via common.compare): the matched assay's recording path DOES detect the object upstream while the LC readouts sit at the null -- Mi4 diff_abs_best_cell_mean +0.0362 +- 0.0033 vs null +0.0173 +- 0.0050, z +3.78, U 25, p 0.008 -> verdict 'result'; Tm3 +0.0945 +- 0.0041 vs +0.0416 +- 0.0153, z +3.46, p 0.008 -> 'result'; LPLC2 diff_max_over_cells_mean_mv +0.6286 +- 0.0524 vs +0.3241 +- 0.1090, z +2.79, U 25, p 0.008 (= p_floor at 5 v 5) -> 'null' only because z < 3, with all five object runs above all five nulls; Mi1 z +2.05, Tm5Y z +1.76, TmY21 z +1.35, T3 abs z +0.96, T2 z +0.65 -> all 'null'. So the new matched assay reproduces round-1's 'carried upstream, attenuated at the LC10/LC11 inputs' pattern without the elevation/speed confound. Note p_floor is 0.008 at 5 v 5, so a 3-rung x 5-run family can be Holm-corrected; a 4 v 4 arm floors at 0.029 and cannot survive Holm over more than one test.

## verify:synthetic  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

(the synthetic radiance stimuli and the RF localizer, `scripts/probe_synthetic_stimuli.py`)

### Refuted
* KEY CLAIM 1 / audit 1.1 'a 4.4-deg rectangle never covers a whole column (max coverage 0.77)': the 0.77 number is wrong for the shipped retina. Measured from the stored radiance (out/synth/rect_dark_h088_w044_radiance.npz, coverage = (r/bg-1)/contrast, channel spread 1e-7): max coverage over the sweep is 0.8853, not 0.77. 0.77 = 0.3118 + 4x0.1147 is only the exactly-centred value (the two rays at daz = +-2.25 fall outside a 4.4-wide window); a 0.05-deg offset admits a sixth ray and gives 0.8853. The qualitative claim survives (max < 1), and 8.8-deg and larger squares do reach exactly 1.0. The cited test (tests/test_object_round2.py:250) only asserts 0.005 < rel.min() < 0.5, so it never pinned 0.77.
* VALIDATION CLAIMED 'SyntheticStimuliTests = 16 tests': the class runs 17 tests (python -m unittest tests.test_object_round2.SyntheticStimuliTests -> 'Ran 17 tests'). The total of 25 is right and all 25 pass in 1.7 s.
* 'the stored radiance IS the presented input, checksum-verified per arm' is false for --null runs. out/synth/null_blank_blank_radiance.npz stores the 4.4x8.8 rect frames under key 'radiance' (226 of 300 frame sums differ from the blank sum) although both arms presented only the constant blank; the stored checksum__blank_a/b equal the blank sum exactly, so the checksums are right, but the provenance string written at scripts/probe_synthetic_stimuli.py:550 ('the stored array IS the presented input') is asserted unconditionally in the null runs' retina record too. Only summary.arms / prov.stimulus.null disambiguate.

### Corrections
* Provenance, material: the cluster batches did not run the committed model. The runs' source_fingerprint gives flyverse/optic.py = 4e3dcca8ae86... which is the hooks task's UNCOMMITTED working-tree file; HEAD's blob is 1ea77ff68817.... flyverse/fly.py (75b93184...) and flyverse/senses.py (67590f95...) on the box match neither HEAD nor the current tree (mid-edit proprioception versions). flyverse_commit reads {'commit': 'unknown', 'dirty': false} - the false is an artefact of there being no .git in the run dir, not evidence of a clean tree, and audit 3 says 'commit unknown' without noting that the content differs from HEAD. I tested the consequence: with the shipped defaults and seed 0 a clean HEAD checkout (git archive HEAD flyverse) and the current tree give bit-identical drive_mv / optic_dr / spike_count over 80 frames of the rect stimulus on CPU (max |diff| 0.0, both arms, all three quantities; a repeat run confirms the path is deterministic). So the numbers are almost certainly unaffected, but every 'shipped lobe' statement here inherits the hooks task's off-path bit-identity claim on the CUDA path (prov shows cuda_kernels true, hook_info.active false).
* Assay power, the biggest substantive gap: the chosen trajectory (elevation 0, azimuth +-30 deg) runs through the sparsest patch of the shipped retina. Of 1466 columns only 16 have |el| <= 6 within |az| <= 35, and exactly ONE column lies between az -15 and +15 (at -4.3); the 10x10-deg cells along the equator hold 0-2 columns against 4-8 elsewhere. Measured consequences from the stored radiance: the 4.4x8.8 dark rectangle dims NO column at all (max coverage < 1e-6) in 74 of 300 frames and dims a median of 0.23 column-equivalents; the 8.8-deg square is invisible in 26 of 300 frames (median 0.77 column-equivalents); the 8.8-deg ON/OFF flash at (0,0) ever touches 2 columns above 5 % coverage, peaks at 0.656, and has ONE column above half-max. So 'the small rectangles and the 8.8-deg flashes do not leave the null behind' is partly a statement about stimulus placement in a retinal hole, not about LC11. Audit 7.1 discusses the localizer's power but nothing flags this for the families or the flashes; the ladder batch inherits the same trajectory. The localizer nodes are far better placed (median 1.0 / 4.0 / 11.4 column-equivalents at 4.5 / 8.8 / 15 deg).
* Spectral aliasing not flagged: the 32.09-32.11 Hz line is measured from a series sampled once per 10-ms frame (100 Hz), while the lobe integrates at dt_ms 1. A true line at 67.9 or 132.1 Hz would alias onto 32.1 Hz, so '32 Hz at LC10a/Tm5Y/TmY21' should read 'a sharp line at 32.1 Hz modulo the 100-Hz frame sampling'. The 4.4-4.8 Hz line is well below Nyquist and is safe.
* Scope of 'LC11 stays at zero at every size': the sizes actually run are 4.5 deg (shipped x3 runs + fb0 x1), 8.8 deg x3 passes (fb0 and shipped, one run each) and 15 deg x3 passes (fb0 only, one run). The 15-deg case - the one where LC10a first shows retinotopy - was never run on the shipped lobe, and the 13-LC10a-body result is a single run of the non-shipped gain_fb=0 configuration. The audit's tables label this correctly; the report's headline sentence drops the 'one run, gain_fb=0' qualifier.
* Replicate discipline: every family number and every batch-2 RF map is n = 1 run per arm, against a round rule of >= 5 runs per arm in one submission. The report and audit say 'magnitudes, no verdicts' and use no compare() verdict vocabulary, which is the right call - but the three-run shipped localizer is the only multi-run measurement in the build, and the LC10a-at-15-deg and 'oscillation is sustained' statements each rest on one run (the latter is at least a 143-s record with internal thirds).

## verify:hooks  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

(the opt-in per-stream optic hooks, `flyverse/optic.py` + `scripts/probe_object_hooks.py`)

### Refuted
* "9x the base, 13x the null" (docs/audits/optic_stream_hooks.md section 5; REPORT headline) OVERSTATES the rectify effect, because the batch has no hook-matched null: every arm is scored against the SHIPPED model's none-vs-none null, and the rectify hook raises its own null 2.6x. I ran the missing control through their own documented CLI (probe_object_hooks.py record --arm null --optic "stream_rectify=^(Mi1|Tm3|Tm2)$,^T3$,pos;^(Tm1|Tm4)$,^T3$,neg", 3 runs, vast-b/B200, out/skeptic_hooks/rectnull_r{0,1,2}.json; hook_info rr_matched 26,833 / rr_rest 8,752,204 and W md5 ef23cc27bea13be7f6a96f3c04fd3737 identical to their runs). T3 diff_signed_best_cell, rectify NONE-vs-NONE = 0.0087 +- 0.0020 [0.0101, 0.0097, 0.0064] vs the shipped null 0.0034 +- 0.0013. So rectify's 0.0451 +- 0.0023 is 5.19x its OWN null (common.compare U 9, p 0.10, z 17.9, verdict underpowered), not 13.4x. The direction and the U 9/9 separation SURVIVE the matched null; the quoted ratio does not.
* "LC11 diff_max_over_cells_mean_mv ... rectify 0.130 +- 0.035 ... null 0.048 +- 0.031" is scored against the wrong null and its rank support disappears under the right one. Against the rectify-MATCHED null (my runs: 0.0726 +- 0.0434 [0.0716, 0.1165, 0.0298]) it is 1.80x with U 8.0, p 0.2, z 1.33 -- overlapping runs, no rank separation -- where against the shipped null the build reports 2.72x, U 9.0, p 0.10, z 2.63. The audit doc already calls this "a point estimate that the scatter covers"; the matched null confirms that and removes the U 9/9 support the shipped-null comparison appeared to give it.
* "adapt (100 ms, gain 1) and suppress (k 0.5, 10 deg) are live (levels move: ... LC11 rate_hz_mean 0.0031 -> 0.0008 / 0.0006)" and "LC11 firing unchanged (rate_hz_mean 0.0035 vs 0.0031 Hz)" rest on statistics at the counting floor. LC11 has n_cells = 143 and a 12 s scored window, so rate_hz_mean x 143 x 12 s is the whole-population spike count: base 5.3 spikes [5, 5, 6], rectify 6.0 [6, 7, 5], adapt 1.3 [2, 0, 2], suppress 1.0 [2, 0, 1], null 2.7 [3, 3, 2]. "Firing unchanged" is 6 vs 5 spikes; the adapt/suppress "level move" is 5 spikes -> 1. cells_over_1hz is 0 in every arm. These carry no information and must not be cited as liveness evidence; liveness is already established by hook_info and by T3 dev_abs_mean (0.0838 -> 0.0804 / 0.0788), which are real.

### Corrections
* SCOPE: "the shipped defaults are bit-identical" must be read as 'on CPU / on a deterministic backend'. It cannot be tested on CUDA at all. My control on a B200 (out/skeptic_hooks2/verify.json): the SAME default lobe object replayed twice differs by max 2.098e-05 (cuda_kernels=True/warp) and 2.480e-05 (cuda_kernels=False/torch sparse); two separate builds of the same code differ by 3.052e-05 / 5.341e-05; worktree-vs-HEAD differs by 1.907e-05 / 1.144e-05 -- i.e. BELOW the same-object non-determinism floor. There is therefore no GPU evidence against the claim and none obtainable. This is a pre-existing property of the shipped pipeline (consistent with the round-1 fact that the figure stage is not fixed-seed reproducible), not something the hooks introduced.
* UNMEASURED CONFOUND, now measured and immaterial: every hook arm runs the Torch substep while base and null run the native CUDA optic kernels, so base-vs-hook confounds the hook with a backend switch. The build did not quantify this. I did: a no-match hook (stream_rectify=[('^NoSuchType$','^T3$','pos')], Torch substep, split matrices, 0 matched entries) vs the native CUDA default differs by max 1.335e-05 -- below the 2.098e-05 same-object floor. The backend switch is not a material confound at these magnitudes. Worth recording in the audit doc.
* "an inhibitory entry never excites" is TRUE but VACUOUS on the configuration the batch actually ran: all 26,833 Mi1/Tm3/Tm2/Tm1/Tm4 -> T3 entries are excitatory (inhibitory_entries = 0 in my full-lobe check, all three modes). The inhibitory guarantee is exercised only on the synthetic Pm1(GABA)->T3 subset in tests/test_optic_hooks.py. The load-bearing fact on the real stream is the other half of the same test: the LINEAR sum flips sign on 13,354 of those 26,833 excitatory entries (presynaptic cells below baseline disinhibiting), and the rectifier is precisely what removes that. Neurome's constraint ("rectification must never turn inhibitory input excitatory") is satisfied structurally by x >= 0, but this batch tests it on no inhibitory synapse.
* The 'neg' mode preserves the sign of W but INVERTS the sign of the signal: an excitatory synapse whose presynaptic cell drops below baseline now produces excitation. This is the substantive modelling assumption behind the whole rectify result and it is NOT a per-synapse-plausible mechanism -- it is a stand-in for an unmodelled OFF pathway. To the build's credit the audit doc states this explicitly ("the OFF transition signalled as excitation through an excitatory synapse ... a hypothesis about the presynaptic transfer, not a fact of the connectome, and the arm exists so it can fail"), but the REPORT's one-line framing ("synaptic signs preserved") understates it and should not go to Neurome without the doc's caveat.
* The liveness magnitudes are measured on probe_object_sweep's protocol -- the exact geometry Neurome asked be replaced. The in-run radiance_check shows elevation varying from -1.49 to +10.01 deg and azimuth sweeping 51.4 -> 2.4 deg within a run, i.e. the size/position/speed confound of Neurome point 3 is fully present. The audit doc says so and defers the model comparison to the matched assay, but the T3 = 0.0451 figure inherits those confounds and must not be quoted as a size-tuning result.
* Neurome's request for a TIE-AWARE U was not implemented. common.compare calls scipy.stats.mannwhitneyu(..., method='exact') for n<=40, which is not tie-aware, and 26 of the 234 comparisons in object_hooks.json contain ties (e.g. LC11 diff_rate_hz_max_cell rectify = 0.083 on all three runs). Nothing rests on it here because every verdict is 'underpowered', but the request is still open.
* flyverse/interp/common.py::parse_kv is a SHARED file this round (probe_object_matched.py and probe_synthetic_stimuli.py also use it). The change is additive and backward-compatible (verified by the build's own assertion and by the 120-test regression), but the grammar is broader than the report states: a value containing ';' takes the tuple-list grammar under ANY key, not only the five TUPLE_LIST_KEYS. That is documented in the docstring; it is just not in the report's API summary.
* Harness note on my own numbers: my bare OpticLobe (receptor=None) gives W_rr nnz 8,780,774 (26,833 matched + 8,753,941 rest) against their 8,779,037 (26,833 + 8,752,204). The difference is entirely because their runs build through Fly with receptor_model='sign' (provenance model.lif.receptor_model), which rewrites the optic edge values. Their split arithmetic is internally exact, and my cluster runs through their own CLI reproduced 26,833 / 8,752,204 to the entry. This is not a discrepancy in their reporting.
* Files I added (no file of the build was edited): D:\Projects\flyverse\scripts\skeptic_hooks_verify.py and D:\Projects\flyverse\scripts\_skeptic_optic_head_ref.txt (generators); D:\Projects\flyverse\out\skeptic_hooks\rectnull_r{0,1,2}.json/.txt (the hook-matched null, 3 runs, B200), D:\Projects\flyverse\out\skeptic_hooks2\verify.json/.txt (the full-lobe GPU verification), D:\Projects\flyverse\out\skeptic_hooks_cluster.log and out\skeptic_hooks2_cluster.log. Caveat on my own runs: the scheduler flagged the verify job "failed exit 0" in both submissions (it appears to flag on stderr warnings); the second run nevertheless completed and wrote a complete verify.json, which is what I quote. The three rectnull jobs completed exit 0 on vast-b.
* RECOMMENDED BEFORE THIS GOES TO NEUROME: (1) add a per-hook matched null to the arm set (arm null + the arm's own --optic override) -- it costs 3 jobs per hook and it is the difference between 13x and 5.2x; (2) restate the LC11 result as 1.8x its own null with U 8 / p 0.2 rather than 2.7x with U 9; (3) drop LC11 rate_hz_mean from the liveness evidence and cite hook_info and T3 dev_abs_mean instead; (4) record the measured Torch-substep-vs-CUDA-kernel delta (1.34e-05, below the 2.10e-05 same-object floor) in the audit doc so the backend switch is documented as checked; (5) qualify 'bit-identical' as CPU/deterministic-backend in the report, as the doc's test section already implies.

The round-2 BATCH threads (`build:baseline` = the matched sphere ladder, `build:compare` = the fixed-anatomy
model comparison, and the exporter pass) carry their own skeptic verdicts (`verify:baseline` **mostly
sound**, `verify:compare` **mostly sound**), appended verbatim below. **The export task had no separate
skeptic**: no `verify:export` was run, so the export's corrections come from this round's critic and are
recorded in `object_export_r2.md` 3 and 4 (only 14 of 84 consoles arrived, so the device claim rests on the
run JSONs; and the ladder summary's `preference.csv` shipped the refuted `spearman_perm` floor, re-emitted
2026-09-14) rather than under a verdict heading here.

One item below is applied to this round's code rather than only recorded: `verify:compare` refuted the
'isolated ON / OFF transitions' label on the flash battery (the stimulus is a periodic square, so both
polarities are inside every run and the whole-window statistic pools them), which is fixed analysis-side in
`scripts/probe_synthetic_stimuli.py` and documented in `object_synthetic_stimuli.md` 6.1; the split was
afterwards regenerated on CPU from the stored recordings (`out/interp/objr2c/spec_transitions.json`,
`object_compare_r2.md` 6.1).

Both verdicts were written **before** the batches were analysable -- the skeptics read the design, the
queued jobs and the code, not the results -- so their refutations are predictions. Every one of them landed:
the `spearman_perm` floor did fire on a primary preference test, no `0 failed` line exists for either batch,
arm was confounded with cluster box, and the flash battery measured bright-vs-dark rather than ON-vs-OFF.

## verify:baseline  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

(the matched sphere ladder batch, `scripts/object_round2_baseline.py`; `docs/audits/object_baseline_r2.md`)

### Refuted
* REFUTED (implicit in the ANSWER: the pipeline "produces the predeclared statistics ... Spearman/contrast permutation tests"): `spearman_perm` (scripts/object_round2_baseline.py:181-191) returns the SMALLEST ATTAINABLE permutation p whenever rho is undefined. `cnt += abs(rho_perm) >= abs(rho) - 1e-12` is False for every shuffle when rho is NaN, so cnt=0 and p = (0+1)/(20000+1) = 4.99975e-05. In the smoke this fired on 12 of 24 rows of table `sphere_preference` (spearman_rho None, spearman_p_perm 0.00005) and on 8 rows of the null trend test (null_spearman_rho NaN, null_spearman_p_perm 0.0005 = 1/2001). Exposure in the real batch is a PREDECLARED PRIMARY test: the smoke shows every LC population spike median is exactly 0.0 in every run (both arms, both types), so if that holds at 6x6 the spikes_median rho is NaN across all 36 object runs and the shipped script will print `drive/spikes preference p_perm = 5e-05` on a primary preference test. Fix before analysis: return NaN when rho is not finite. `contrast_perm` (:194-202) is safe (NaN when an arm is < 2, p = 1.0 when pooled values are constant).
* REFUTED (KEY CLAIM 3, last clause: "so '<n> job(s), 0 failed' will be meaningful"): that line will never exist for this batch. out/objr2_cluster.log is still 0 bytes and NO cluster_run client for objr2 is alive locally (Win32_Process enumeration: only `cluster_run.py --name d2-hold` (pid 44116) and `--name vncd` (pid 29536) from other tasks are running). `verify_all` (:394-399) requires the log to contain ", 0 failed" and `cmd_analyse` (:848-852) calls `sys.exit("batch verification failed")` unless `--force`. The per-job `check-job` gate still runs ON THE BOX and still exits 1 correctly; what is lost is the client-side aggregate, so the eventual analysis must be run with --force and the Result will carry the problem string.
* REFUTED as a current status (STATUS AT THE FORCED CLOSE: "all 16 jobs were QUEUED ... no process of this batch had started"): true at the 21:46 stamp, false now. The batch is running. Live count of written summary JSONs in /root/runs/objr2-6915ce/out/objr2 across the four boxes: vast-d 25, vast-a 5, vast-b 0, vast-c 0 = 30 of 238 processes (~13 %), roughly 70 min after submission; vast-b and vast-c are still behind other tasks' jobs. Still NOTHING analysable -- no arm has its 6 runs (only sphere seeds s0/s1 exist), so the ANSWER's core statement (no size-tuning number exists) stands.

### Corrections
* KEY CLAIM 5 needs one addition: scripts/object_round2_baseline.py ITSELF was edited AFTER the predeclaration stamp and after submission began (mtime 2026-09-13T21:46:53Z vs predeclared.json 21:45:39Z, submit_console 21:46:06Z). The box copy (sha256 95d3266de2ca...) differs from the local copy (de7c1e4b7397...), and out/objr2/tree_state.json records the box hash, so tree_state no longer describes the local tree. I fetched the box copy and diffed: 15 lines, both hunks in analysis-only code -- `compare_row`'s empty-arm branch rewritten to use common.ArmStats, and a duplicate `n_changed_5pct_mean` key removed from `contrast_readout` (it collided with pom.footprint's key used at :630). `build_jobs`, `cmd_run_job` and the process command strings are byte-identical, so the running batch is unaffected. But 'stamped before submission' covers the reading rules, not the analysis code, and the eventual analysis will be run by a script the boxes never saw.
* KEY CLAIM 6: '924-assignment tie-aware exact U' describes the 6v6 design, not the smoke. The smoke arms are 5 v 5, so the enumeration was C(10,5) = 252 assignments; the quoted agreement '0.5476 = 0.5476' and the disagreement '0.0794 vs 0.0556' were both established at 252. `tie_aware_exact_u` enumerates C(n_a+n_b, n_a) exactly up to max_enum 200000 and falls back to a 200k Monte Carlo above that, so 924 will indeed be exact at 6v6.
* KEY CLAIM 4 (WinError 206) is plausible but mis-attributed. No single job line would have exceeded the limit: inlining the 238 process commands gives a longest job line of 6,628 characters. What overflows is the SINGLE argv of the `python scripts/cluster_run.py ...` subprocess, which carries all 16 job lines: 57,956 characters inlined vs 4,387 with the run-job indirection, against the 32,767-character CreateProcess limit. The conclusion (the indirection was necessary) holds; the stated cause does not. The failure itself is unverifiable -- no log of the attempt was kept.
* KEY CLAIM 3: `cmd_run_job` (:281-286) captures each process's return code into `rc` and PRINTS it but never acts on it -- a failed process does not abort the job, and later processes still run. The only real gate is the terminal `check-job`, which catches the consequence (missing file / no 'device cuda' / 'Traceback') rather than the exit code. Not a defect in the data, but the job-level 'fails fast' reading of claim 3 is wrong.
* Multiplicity caveat the report does not state: the 12 members of each primary family share the SAME 6 blank/blank runs (each re-windowed with the rung's diameter), and all 24 synthetic rectangle arms share ONE set of 6 null runs. Holm controls FWER under arbitrary dependence so the procedure is valid, but the members are strongly dependent and the report should say so when it quotes the family.
* Design cost worth flagging before unblinding: 6 of the 12 primary members are the spikes_median members, and the smoke shows every LC population spike median is exactly 0.0 on both arms in every run. If that holds at 6x6 those 6 members are structurally uninformative yet double the Holm denominator -- which is the entire reason the arm size had to go from 5 to 6. A drive-only 6-member family needs p <= 0.05/6 = 0.00833, which IS reachable at 5 v 5 (floor 0.00794). Changing this now would be post-hoc; it should be predeclared for the next round.
* Operational, not in the report: the fetch is ~28 GB. Each process writes a ~114 MB .npz plus a ~9.7 MB .json (vast-d: 2.5 GB for 21 processes = ~119 MB/process), so `--fetch out/objr2/` over the ssh tunnels moves ~238 x 119 MB. Nothing in the plan trims the npz. When fetching by hand, pull the .json and .txt first (analysis needs the npz only for the per-body windowed drive, footprint and time courses) or prune on the box.
* Answering the refute question directly: the specificity battery was NOT scored for every arm, and could not have been -- it is not in this batch. jobs.json's synthetic_families are hlad_dark, wlad_dark, sqlad_dark, sqlad_bright only. Bars, gratings, stationary flicker and isolated ON/OFF transitions exist only in out/synth (12 runs, 1 seed each, from the synthetic-stimuli task) and are not arms here. This matches the predeclared scope (experiment 1 is the assay; the bright square ladder is the contrast-matched bright control) and Neurome's recommended order, where the specificity controls belong to the model-comparison phase -- but it should be stated rather than left implied.
* 'Rerun the decisive arm with fresh seeds' was not attempted, deliberately. There is no decisive arm (no arm has 6 runs; 30 of 238 processes are done), and submitting a competing batch would contend for the same four boxes that the primary batch is still queued on at vast-b and vast-c, and would risk a second client against the same run directory -- both explicitly against the cluster rules. The right next action is to wait, then fetch by hand per box with `scp -P <port> -r root@<ip>:/root/runs/objr2-6915ce/out/objr2/. out/objr2/` (vast-a 208.64.254.185:28496, vast-b 208.64.254.185:6400, vast-c 145.241.108.98:40269, vast-d 84.8.116.228:40014), run `object_round2_baseline.py verify`, and analyse with --force after fixing the spearman_perm floor bug.

## verify:compare  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

(the fixed-anatomy model comparison batch, `scripts/object_round2_compare.py`; `docs/audits/object_compare_r2.md`)

### Refuted
* 'isolated ON and OFF 15-deg flashes' (spec battery description, and the labels in scripts/object_round2_compare.py:106-107 'isolated ON transitions' / 'isolated OFF transitions'): REFUTED by the stimulus's own code. scripts/probe_synthetic_stimuli.py:241-256 flash() is a PERIODIC square (--on-s 0.5 --period-s 1.5 over 6 s = 4 cycles), and its docstring plus the recorded params['transition'] say 'ON at onset / OFF at offset' for a bright square and 'OFF at onset / ON at offset' for a dark one -- every run contains BOTH transition polarities. Worse, scripts/probe_synthetic_stimuli.py:581 calls family_stats(recs['stim'], recs['blank']) with window=None, i.e. the WHOLE-window time mean, so the two edges are pooled inside each run. The flashon/flashoff pair therefore separates BRIGHT from DARK flashes, not ON from OFF transitions, and the predeclared rule 'T2 and T3 KEEP both transitions' (predeclared.json specificity.on_off) does not measure what its name says. Neurome asked specifically for 'isolated ON / OFF transitions'. Fixable without re-planning: family_stats already takes a `window` argument and an on/off role split already exists for the localizer (NodeAccumulator, probe_synthetic_stimuli.py:425-440).
* 'predeclared the reading rules BEFORE submission (out/objr2c/predeclared.json; docs/audits/object_compare_r2.md sections 0-2)': the second half is false. out/objr2c/predeclared.json mtime 2026-09-13 15:04:04.62 and out/objr2c/submit_console.txt 15:06:37.61, so the JSON stamp genuinely precedes submission -- but docs/audits/object_compare_r2.md has mtime 15:07:27, AFTER the batch was submitted. The substance is unaffected (I loaded the current PREDECLARED and ARMS dicts from scripts/object_round2_compare.py and they are byte-identical to the stamped out/objr2c/predeclared.json, so nothing was rewritten), but the audit doc is not the predeclaration stamp and should not be cited as one.

### Corrections
* ARM IS FULLY CONFOUNDED WITH CLUSTER BOX -- the most serious unhandled defect in the queued design, and it affects the PRIMARY question. Each arm's entire sphere set is one job (jobs.json: sph_base ... sph_rect_supp, 30 processes each), scheduled least-loaded-first, so submit_console.txt shows sph_base@vast-a, sph_fb0@vast-b, sph_rectify@vast-d, sph_adapt100@vast-a, sph_suppress@vast-d, ... Predeclared question (ii) 'arm object runs vs the base arm's object runs' therefore compares arm-on-box-X against base-on-box-Y, on different GPUs (the fleet mixes B200 and H200 -- verify.json's own smoke shows both device names in one 20-run set). verify() records device_name per run (object_round2_compare.py:406, and the `device` column of sphere_per_run) but adds NO problem when an arm and base differ, and the comparison carries no device term or stratification. Given the brief's own note that the figure-stage pipeline is not fixed-seed reproducible (every z +-1.5), a `result` on question (ii) could be carrying the box rather than the mechanism. Before reading any arm-vs-base row: check the device column per arm, and if arms landed on different boxes, either re-run base on each box that hosted a hook arm (5 runs, same submission) or restrict every arm-vs-base call to same-device pairs. Question (i) (arm vs the arm's OWN null, same job, same box) is not affected.
* Holm's family shrinks silently on dropout. object_round2_baseline.holm (lines 152-154) drops NaN from m by design ('NaN stays NaN and is not counted in m'). The smoke shows the consequence: the declared 3-member family with 2 empty rungs was adjusted at m=1, giving p_holm 0.5476 rather than 3x0.5476. In the intended complete batch every small rung has 5 runs so m=3 and the predeclared threshold 0.05/3 holds -- but if any rung's processes fail on the cluster, the declared 3-member family quietly becomes a 1- or 2-member family and the threshold loosens. Fix: fix m at the declared 3 (or refuse the call and read `underpowered`) whenever a declared member is missing, and say in the Result which rungs actually contributed.
* The PRIMARY P1 statistic is a max over cells, which is the statistic family Neurome asked to stop leading with. diff_signed_best_cell is defined at scripts/probe_object_matched.py:252 as float(np.abs(ma - mb).max()) -- max over cells of |mean_t dr_A - mean_t dr_B|, positive-definite, whose null is an extremum over thousands of cells. The predeclared text names this definition honestly and the design does carry per-body material alongside (sphere_time_course, the RF-windowed abs_drive_median, per_body_sphere.csv), and P2 (LC11) IS correctly a per-body windowed median. Still: when the batch lands, report the per-body windowed T3/T2 medians beside P1 and say plainly that P1 is a within-run population maximum, or Neurome's round-1 objection returns on the round-2 headline.
* The three members of each primary vs-null family are not independent. Each arm has only 5 blank/blank null runs, and those same 5 are the reference at all three rungs (for the upstream table the null rows carry diam_deg NaN and fall through to the whole null set, object_round2_compare.py:577; for the LC table the 5 null runs are replicated into every rung by the `for dd in (DIAMS if nm['null'] else [nm['diam']])` loop at line 521). Holm is valid under arbitrary dependence so the adjustment itself is sound, but the family must not be described as three independent tests when the report is written.
* Read the fb0 arm's vs-null rows with care beyond 'undetermined'. The predeclared fb0 clause counts an `undetermined` vs-null row as a figure whenever diff > 0 (object_round2_compare.py:607), i.e. a sign check against a zero-SD null with no p at all. That is defensible for a deterministic lobe, but it is a strictly weaker bar than every other arm's, and it should be reported as a magnitude with the 5-run scatter rather than tabulated in the same column as the Holm-surviving calls.
* Operationally, for whoever picks this up: the batch is still live and queued on vast-a/b/c/d under run id objr2c-a9af1d (the run dirs and the shipped 35 files are in place on all four boxes; out/ and logs/ are empty). Fetch by hand per .cluster.json with 'scp -P <port> -r root@<ip>:/root/runs/objr2c-a9af1d/out/objr2c/. out/objr2c/' from each of the four boxes and merge, then run verify before analyse. Do not point a second client at out/objr2c/ while a fetch is in flight.
